‘Facing The Numbers:’ Kieran Satour & Tyson Perkins On Telling An Authentic First Nations Story With Their Breakout Hybrid Feature

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Facing The Numbers by Kieran Satour and Tyson Perkins is one of the most unique propositions to come out of Australia in some time.

Described as a “social impact campaign and film project,” the feature-length documentary explores the significant disadvantages First Nations people experience in Australia’s legal system.

Recent data show that First Nations people experience disadvantages across all areas of Aussie life, including child protection, justice, health, housing and education. First Nations people make up around 3.3% of the general population, but 37% of the prison population. Related Stories News 'Dune: Part Three' Stars Set For Very Childish BBC Prank Tonight Politics Blue Wave Coming For Chuck Schumer? Chris Van Hollen To Challenge Him For Senate Majority Leadership

The data is stark, but as the doc’s title suggests, Satour and Perkins are interested in more than just listing these figures. Watch on Deadline

“The statistics tell one story. We tell another,” the film’s opening frame reads.

Structured as a six-part anthology, Facing The Numbers explores these disparities through the real-life stories of First Nations people using a bold mix of dance, poetry, and spoken word.

Satour and Perkins wrote and directed the film together. Perkins also served as the DoP. Satour produced alongside Ramona Telecican. The film debuted earlier this year at the Melbourne Film Festival before screening across the Aussie circuit and premiering at the Sydney Opera House. The doc will screen on NITV in Australia on November 15 and will later be repackaged for distribution on social platforms.

Below, Satour and Perkins speak with Deadline about how they developed the anthology film and their bold plans for its release.

DEADLINE: This film has a very unique structure. How did you develop this approach to the material?

TYSON PERKINS: This kind of subject matter is often portrayed in a very academic way on film. It tends to take the form of talking-head interviews and fairly basic re-creations, which don’t get close to the emotional point of view—or the emotional suffering—that many victims of police brutality go through. We started with that as our aim, and this little, unusual thing came out of it.

DEADLINE: I read this started as a short project and then expanded. Is that right?

PERKINS: It was as much about treating the subject matter in a fresh way as it was about using a fresh format. For us, that format was short-form and shareable online. It came as a response to the Black Lives Matter movement. We were watching what was happening in America from Australia and the artistic responses that came out of it—not the news coverage, but the things that appeared on our Instagram feeds. It prompted Kieran and me to think about Australia. Some of our statistics on incarceration, youth incarceration, male suicide, and deaths in custody are among the worst in the world. We wanted to take some of those subjects and statistics and frame them through the short-form, viral storytelling many African American artists in the U.S. were using. By “viral,” we didn’t necessarily mean that it had to go viral; we meant something short, shareable, and to the point.

As happens with films, once we delved into the individual subjects and the stories, they expanded. We realised there was a way to tell the story of this country—its institutional racism, its history of oppression and its ongoing oppression—through these personal, first-hand stories. We thought we had six separate subject films, but when we put them together, they formed a truthful picture of what Aboriginal people face in this country.

DEADLINE: You incorporate several different art forms in the film. There’s dance, spoken word. But it feels seamless. How did you get it to work?

KIERAN SATOUR: One of the amazing things about Facing the Numbers is that so many First Nations artists came together to tell this story. The spoken word, poetry, and dancing feel seamless because everyone is telling the same story. We’re telling multiple separate stories, but they represent a collective Aboriginal experience. Tyson and I were lucky to work with all these incredible creatives.

DEADLINE: How have people been responding to the film, especially in Australia?

SATOUR: People have been responding really well. We premiered at the Melbourne International Film Festival and had a great turnout. About 75% of tickets were sold for both screenings. We had standing ovations, and the Letterboxd reviews came in almost immediately after the first screening. Nothing was under four stars, and the reviews were glowing. We screened at the Darwin International Film Festival. My sister lives in Darwin. She’s also a co-founder of the production company and an executive producer on the film. She told me that people have been stopping her in Darwin to talk about Facing the Numbers because they’d been at the screening. It’s been amazing.

DEADLINE: Where did you find your participants?

SATOUR: During the global movement after George Floyd’s murder, people were sharing their stories online, and we found some of these stories through social media. Geoffrey Johnson is one example. Otherwise, we were doing research and development, trying to find stories that spoke to this collective experience. It came down to meetings and phone calls.

PERKINS: It’s important to mention that it would not be difficult to find thousands of stories like these. Thousands of Aboriginal people have direct descendants who were taken during the Stolen Generations or sent to schools like Kinchela Boys’ Home. Probably every Aboriginal family knows someone who has been a victim of police brutality, whether in their own family or a family they know. The stories we chose are told by extremely courageous people. It takes strength to repeat your story on camera, allow it to be filmed, and make it public. But one of the key messages of the film is that these experiences are extraordinarily common—in people’s lives and statistically.

DEADLINE: How did you build this production? I can’t imagine the Australian public funding system was jumping to invest in something like this. Or maybe I’m wrong.

SATOUR: It was funded through a digital-series, online-production fund at Screen Australia, our national public screen-funding body. That fund doesn’t even exist anymore. The project was never meant to be a feature film. We made the anthology into a feature partly because that helped us unlock extra funding and become eligible for a significant tax offset. Production went through a few phases. At the end of the first phase, we had two strong rough cuts, which we used to secure more funding. We received a little more from the national broadcasting funding body, then a broadcaster attached through a pre-sale, and we brought in philanthropic support. It was a struggle, but we got there. A film like this doesn’t get made without the goodwill of philanthropists, but also without all the work everyone put into those first two rough cuts. They were strong, and I think the project sold itself from there.

DEADLINE: How will it be distributed? I know you’re screening at the Sydney Opera House, which is wonderful. Do you have plans for international screenings? In the UK and U.S., people involved in solidarity movements would be intrigued to hear from you and see a film like this.

SATOUR: Totally. In terms of craft, we were inspired by global Black filmmakers. Steve McQueen’s Small Axe was a huge reference for us, as was Spike Lee. We both admire the American director Khalil Joseph. We’re connecting with global Black and brown movements because we’re facing similar issues everywhere: over-policing, incarceration, and child removal. We’re still waiting to find out where the international premiere will be. In Australia, we’re doing a film-festival run, and then the film will be broadcast on NITV, the National Indigenous Television Network, which sits under SBS, our multicultural public broadcaster. The digital release will follow once the holdbacks for the national TV broadcast are over. The films will also be released online through a microsite.

A key partner is Common Ground, an Aboriginal-run and Aboriginal-led organisation focused on education. They’re running our impact campaign. The organisation’s founder, Rona Glynn-McDonald, is Warwick Thornton’s daughter, so filmmaking is in her blood. We’re excited to get these stories out digitally and reach younger people who may not watch television or attend film festivals, and to connect with an audience that isn’t already converted.

PERKINS: For me, the online release is what I’m most excited about. In film and television now, you see people who have built audiences on YouTube or Instagram bringing those audiences into more traditional film and TV. The ideal outcome would be for some of the storytellers to experience justice—whether for a family member’s death or, in Uncle Widdy’s case, by having more of the public learn what happened to him at Kinchela Boys’ Home. Not many people know that thousands of children went through that school or what happened to them. The survivors are trying to reclaim the land where the school stood. Once the films have been shared and completed their festival runs, I hope some of those outcomes can begin to happen. I’m really excited about that.

UN watchdog 'increasingly concerned' over Iran nuclear site

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The nuclear agency has not been able to inspect any sites attacked by the US and Israel last year

The UN's nuclear watchdog has said it is "increasingly concerned" about a nuclear facility in Iran which it has not been allowed to inspect.

Iran informed the International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) of plans to set up the uranium enrichment plant in the city of Isfahan a day before Israel began attacking its nuclear sites last June. The US joined the strikes later that month.

In a report seen by news agencies, the IAEA said it does not know the exact location or whether it contains nuclear material.

Since the start of the latest war, Iran has restricted IAEA inspectors' access to its nuclear sites, preventing the agency from verifying its enriched uranium stockpile.

Under a 2015 nuclear deal, Iran agreed to limit its enriched uranium stockpile to 300kg and enrichment to 3.67% purity. It subsequently exceeded both.

Before the June 2025 strikes, it had accumulated an estimated 441kg enriched to 60%, close to weapons grade.

The nuclear complex at Isfahan suffered extensive damage in US and Israeli strikes, particularly the parts above the ground. The condition of its underground facilities is not clear.

The IAEA believes much of Iran's highly enriched uranium was stored there.

"The Agency's lack of information about [Iran's] nuclear material and access to facilities to verify it is a matter of serious proliferation concern... and needs to be rectified with the utmost urgency," it said in the latest report.

Iran announced plans for the Isfahan Fuel Enrichment Plant (IFEP) on 12 June 2025, shortly after an IAEA resolution which declared Iran had failed to meet its nuclear safeguards obligations.

Iran initially agreed to allow inspectors to visit the facility, but the inspection was cancelled after Israel launched attacks the following day.

The IAEA's latest calls come almost a year after it made a similar appeal to Iran. At the time it said it had only inspected some of the 13 nuclear facilities that were "unaffected" by the attacks and none of the seven that were.

The US and Israel said they attacked Iran's nuclear sites to prevent it developing nuclear weapons - something Iran has consistently denied seeking. It says its nuclear programme is for peaceful civilian purposes only.

How Manchester United could line up in Mauricio Pochettino’s first game vs Liverpool

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino is officially set to become the next Manchester United manager, and his first game will come just days after his start date.

The Red Devils return from the final international break of 2026 to face the daunting prospect of visiting arch-rivals Liverpool in the Premier League on Sunday, November 22.

Pochettino inherits a side with just one win in 10 top-flight games this term, a string of results that have left Man Utd in the relegation zone.

The Argentine’s style of play centres around a high-press and an ability to build from the back - two things United have struggled with so far this season.

Injuries across the pitch will impact Pochettino’s selection, with Carlos Baleba and Benjamin Sesko definitely ruled out for the trip to Anfield.

In goal, Senne Lammens is established first-choice and that is unlikely to change with Pochettino’s arrival.

Interestingly, Pochettino might be tempted to copy Carrick and play Leny Yoro at right-back. Out of possession the right-back tucks in to form a three-man defence in Pochettino’s favoured system, but with an actual left-back fit, there is little reason to play defenders out of position.

For that reason, Diogo Dalot will likely start at right-back, with a much more simple defensive approach. Yoro and Ayden Heaven in the heart of defence could give United a bit more pace than a backline featuring Harry Maguire, but the experience of Lisandro Martinez may be hard to ignore as Pochettino balances a squad lacking in confidence.

Patrick Dorgu seems a strong fit at left-back. Antonee Robinson loved to get forward for Pochettino while on USA duty, and the wing-back is a proven attacking threat.

With the USA, Pochettino has settled on a 4-3-3 formation, with the midfield featuring a sitting player behind two more proactive team-mates.

Man Utd appoint Mauricio Pochettino as manager on two-and-a-half year deal

Pochettino has already decided his first two Man Utd signings

When will Pochettino start as Man Utd manager?

Pochettino’s immediate issue with the United squad will be the fact that there is no clear candidate as a holding midfielder. The injured Baleba does well at screening the backline, but his strengths rely on driving forward with the ball.

It is a similar story for Andrey Santos - a midfielder that is still unsure whether his best position is as a No6, 8, or 10.

The deep-lying playmaker role is a battle between Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans. Pochettino has shown a tendency to trust young, talented players, so it would not be a surprise to see the former Tottenham manager try and work his magic with Mainoo.

Tielemans could play the box-to-box role alongside Mainoo, offering experience during the transition to Pochettino’s ideal approach.

While Pochettino has utilised a deeper player in the middle of the park with the USA, it is likely that he will instead have to build a more attacking midfield at United.

That is because club captain Bruno Fernandes is clearly the Red Devils’ best player, which merits his inclusion.

Matheus Cunha looks a good fit on the left wing as a creative spark, while Bryan Mbeumo’s pace down the right wing would allow Pochettino to create a similar attacking shape to his USA side.

However, with United’s striking options limited, it would not be a surprise to see Mbeumo lead the line, with Amad Diallo playing instead on the right.

Predicted Manchester United XI vs Liverpool (4-2-3-1): Lammens; Dalot, Yoro, Martinez, Dorgu; Tielemans, Mainoo; Amad, Fernandes, Cunha; Mbeumo

UK, Singapore, and Thailand central banks complete foreign exchange settlement trial linking traditional and tokenized systems

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Reserve Bank of Australia and Bangko Sentral ng Pilipinas participated as observers, while the Bank of England plans to launch a live synchronisation interface in 2028.

Three central banks have completed tests of a cross-border foreign exchange settlement system connecting traditional payment infrastructure with distributed ledger technology.

In a joint statement on Friday, the Bank of England, Monetary Authority of Singapore, and Bank of Thailand said they conducted the experiments using a prototype developed by Partior, which coordinated payment-versus-payment (PvP) settlement and multilateral net FX transactions across their test environments.

The three central banks are working under Project Meridian FX APAC, announced last November. The project encompassed multiple currencies, technologies, settlement infrastructures, and time zones, with the Reserve Bank of Australia and Bangko Sentral ng Pilipinas also participating as observers, the banks said.

According to the statement, Partio's system matched settlement instructions, coordinated the reservation of funds, monitored payment readiness, and initiated settlement.

The experiment also examined multilateral netting, which allows banks to offset payments against one another to reduce the liquidity needed for settlement.

However, the accompanying policy report identified questions around operational resilience, settlement finality, governance, liability, and cross-border data sharing. It also warned that additional functions, including holding funds or calculating net positions, could change an operator's regulatory responsibilities.

Tom Mutton, the BOE's executive director of payments, innovation and fintech, said the project provided "practical evidence of how synchronized settlement could help reduce risk and improve efficiency in cross-border FX transactions."

The BOE plans to launch a live synchronization interface in 2028. Findings from Project Meridian FX APAC will inform further work on synchronized settlement.

Milan, San Donato resta nei piani di RedBird: cosa vuole realizzare Cardinale dopo la scelta di San Siro

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il Milan avrà il suo nuovo stadio a San Siro, ma RedBird non ha intenzione di abbandonare San Donato. Il fondo americano guidato da Gerry Cardinale ha annunciato il lancio di Milan Sports & Entertainment (MSE), una holding destinata a riunire i propri investimenti a Milano nei settori dello sport, delle infrastrutture e dell'intrattenimento. Tra i progetti contenuti nel comunicato di presentazione compare espressamente lo sviluppo di "una nuova destinazione per lo sport e l'intrattenimento dal vivo a San Donato". Una conferma importante, dopo che il club rossonero aveva accantonato il progetto di realizzare il proprio stadio nell'area a sud di Milano, scegliendo invece di procedere insieme all'Inter sul futuro di San Siro.

Al momento RedBird non ha fornito dettagli sul tipo di struttura che intende realizzare a San Donato, né sui tempi o sull'entità dell'investimento. Il comunicato si limita a indicare la volontà di sviluppare una nuova destinazione dedicata allo sport e agli eventi dal vivo, senza precisare quali attività potrebbe ospitare. È comunque un'indicazione significativa sulla strategia del fondo americano, che evidentemente continua a considerare San Donato un'area di interesse per i propri investimenti. Il progetto si affiancherebbe al Generali Stadium, il nuovo impianto da 71.500 posti progettato da Foster + Partners e MANICA, destinato a diventare la casa del Milan e a ospitare anche concerti e altre iniziative commerciali. La nuova holding, affidata all'amministratore delegato Brett Yormark, avrà il compito di coordinare e sviluppare le diverse attività del gruppo a Milano. Tra queste figura anche la creazione di un business dedicato alla gestione di stadi e arene, con particolare attenzione ai servizi di ristorazione, al merchandising e alle suite premium. Non è stato chiarito se e in che misura queste attività potranno riguardare anche San Donato. Più in generale, MSE valuterà una possibile franchigia milanese nella futura NBA Europe e opportunità nel tennis internazionale. Per Cardinale si tratta di declinare in una dimensione cittadina l'esperienza maturata nei settori dello sport e dell'intrattenimento: "Oggi applichiamo questa esperienza su una scala diversa: non a un solo club, a un solo sport o a un solo impianto, ma a un'intera città e a un portfolio che comprende sport, lifestyle e intrattenimento".

Make Your Houseplants a DIY Humidity Tray and Gift Them the Glossiest Leaves for the Holidays

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

What Your Peace Lily Needs in November – 5 Winter Wins for Glossy New Growth and Sparkling Blooms

What Your Hydrangeas Need in November – 5 Ways to Get Plants Winter-Ready and Kickstart Your Best Blooms Ever

5 Easy Fall Wins to Help Your Backyard Birds Survive Winter – How to Beat the Big Chill and Ensure More Visits

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Now the heat’s on in your home, dry air means many houseplants are struggling to stay healthy. A homemade pebble tray is the no-cost solution that brings larger, lusher leaves.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Many leafy plants we now grow as houseplants evolved in hot and humid rainforests which is why, now fall’s here and the heat’s turned on, the dry air in our homes is leading to less-than-lovely foliage. So, if you're wondering how to keep houseplants healthy in winter so you can enjoy lush, glossy leaves, you're not alone. Thankfully, a humidity tray is the quick and easy DIY solution that will speed your houseplants back to happy in time for the holidays, and it won't cost you a dime.

It’s a cruel fact that it’s the plants with the biggest or boldest leaves that struggle most in the dry air of our winter-heated homes: adapting to life in a rainforest is exactly why the foliage is so fabulous in the first place. Beneath a dense forest canopy, sunlight is a precious commodity so some plants like Elephant Ear (Alocasia) grow huge leaves to catch as much light as possible. Others such as peace lilies has foliage densely packed with chlorophyll to absorb as much of the scarce light as possible, and that's what creates their intense, deep green hue. Some like Calathea have purple or red undersides to their leaves precisely so that precious light gets bounced back into the leaf.

So, if you want to enjoy these dramatic leaves at their best, you have to provide these houseplants with the humidity level they need to flourish. Any plant likely suffering from a lack of humidity at this time of the year is easy to spot, starting with any houseplants with brown leaf tips or edges, smaller or discolored leaves, or slow growth. Now, to restore these ailing houseplants to lush, glossy growth, you could treat them to a small humidifier like the Homedics Humidifier from Amazon. Or you could simply DIY a humidity tray with stuff you’ve probably already got in your backyard.

A humidity tray, also called a pebble tray, is a shallow container that’s wider than the plant pot it serves, filled with small pebbles and water. The plant pot sits on the pebbles above the water level so instead of water being pulled up into the potting soil, it evaporates into the air. The higher the heat in your home, and so the drier the air, the more water evaporates to create a humid microclimate around your houseplant.

An old plant saucer is perfect, or if you’d like something more decorative for your houseplant display, look in your kitchen cupboards for a deep plate or shallow dish. Fill this with fine gravel or small pebbles – pea gravel is perfect and you can buy bagged pea gravel pebbles from Home Depot very cheaply if you don’t have a gravel path or driveway to raid.

Then simply stand the plant pot on top of your DIY humidity tray and keep the water topped up, making sure the water level stays below the base of the pot.

Grouping your houseplants together will also help to raise the humidity. Clustering plants together creates an indoor microclimate because the water vapor that’s lost through the leaves diffuses more slowly – and this effect is amplified if even one or two of the grouped houseplants have humidity trays.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Houseplants that have evolved to live in the damp environments of rainforests and woodland are most sensitive to humidity. These common houseplants all thrive in 55-80% humidity and will benefit from a DIY pebble tray:

If you grow any of these houseplants, then a $5 room thermometer like this from Amazon that measures humidity levels as well as temperature, and records the minimum and maximum levels in the past 24 hours, is a really useful tool. You’ll be able to see exactly what the humidity levels are and, if they’re too low, take action before the plant tells you it’s an issue with brown leaf tips. Already made the plant a pebble tray? To raise the humidity further, simply DIY a wider one to increase the surface area of water available to evaporate.

Many more adaptable houseplants won’t be as dramatic about a less-than-ideal humidity level, but add a little more moisture to the air and they’ll grow faster and produce bigger leaves with a better color and shine. Making a DIY pebble tray will help all these plants to flourish:

These plants all thrive in humidity levels of 40-60%. While that's likely within the average humidity of a home in summer, it's a different story in winter. Because cold air can't hold as much water, and home heating dries it further, home moisture levels typically drop to 20-40%. While that lack of humidity isn't enough to kill these adaptable plants, it will certainly prevent them from looking their best.

Leaves with brown tips and outer edges are the most common sign that a houseplant is struggling in low humidity levels and is losing moisture from its foliage faster than the roots can replace it. All plants release water vapor from tiny pores on their leaves and, in dry air, that moisture is sucked out at a faster rate. The plant parts furthest away from the roots supplying the water are the first to run out of moisture, which is why leaf tips are the first to dry out to a crispy brown. At this time of the year, it's very common to see houseplants with long, narrow leaves like spider plants with brown leaf tips. Low humidity is also an easily-avoided reason why peace lily flowers turn brown.

There are other symptoms to look out for too, such as slow growth and smaller new leaves. While many plants naturally produce aerial roots, if you spot a sudden escalation in their number, the houseplant is likely seeking more humidity. And if you find that the potting soil dries out super-quickly after watering, too-dry air may be the cause.

Misting houseplants provide a quick fix but the spike in humidity only lasts for a very short time, whereas a humidity tray delivers constant moisture to create a more permanent microclimate. To match the humidity levels provided by a pebble tray, you’d need to mist houseplants every hour, every day, every night. Misting can also create a breeding ground for fungal and bacterial disease by leaving water sitting on foliage.

In dry or arid zones like Arizona, Nevada, New Mexico or Colorado, your houseplants may need humidity trays topped up year-round. You’ll likely see even plants that don’t require very high air moisture levels developing brown tips, which is a sure sign that they’ll benefit from a humidity tray.

At the other end of the scale, if you live in a high-humidity climate like Louisiana, Mississippi or Florida, only the houseplants that are highly dependent on humidity need a pebble tray – unless your home is equipped with an efficient air-conditioning system that dehumidifies, and dependant on your winter home-heat level, of course. Unnecessary standing water in a warm, damp home puts plants at a higher risk of pests and fungal disease.

Gifting many of your houseplants a DIY humidity tray will help them to flourish in our heated homes through fall and winter, and happy plants grow leaves that are larger and lusher, greener and glossier. Along with a cheap thermometer that displays air moisture percentages, your DIY pebble tray makes it very easy to adjust humidity levels, too. Keep the trays topped up with water for maximum humidity, and gradually reduce if needs be once your home's heat is turned off. You can even leave the trays water-free over summer if natural humidity is high enough in your climate, and let your DIY pebble saucers serve as stylish houseplant decor for a personal touch, until the heat's turned back on.

Making compost from fall leaves takes forever, right? Not if you know this gardener's trick to get it ready in time for spring.

Waterlogged soil is the prime reason potted plants fail in fall and winter, and this speedy solution couldn't be simpler.

10 Great HBO Shows With Endings That Change Everything

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The following article contains spoilers.No matter how many seasons it lasts, what themes it focuses on, or even which genre it exists within, every television series must eventually reckon with how to execute a meaningful finale. Many series opt for a cathartic wind-down, a safe and comfortable ending that offers closure to the audience. However, some series do something a little different, opting to strive for more ambitious conclusions that have a more dramatic effect on viewers.

Many of the series included ended with bold and brash big swings that completely reframed how viewers regarded the show. Others misfired as underwhelming resolutions to iconic titles, the backlash of which completely changed how television as a medium regards the art of delivering a climactic episode and season. All of these finales have made an undeniable impact on the format and its fans, and all stand as noteworthy chapters in HBO’s impressive and extended stay as the flagship brand of television excellence in the 21st century.

It is a conclusive shift that isn’t necessarily restricted to the series finale. After all, a tone of realism and uncertainty permeates the entire five-season run, but The Wire’s conclusion does reframe the series quite substantially. Revolving around the complex criminal ecosystem of Baltimore, spanning from the street-level drug dealers to the politics of City Hall, The Wire delivers an all-encompassing, novelistic epic of modern American crime, one laced with dozens of focal characters on both sides of the law.

Towards the end of the final season, it begins to become clear that creator David Simon has no intention of wrapping character arcs up in a neat little bow that will give viewers a sense of closure. Instead, The Wire ends as a grim meditation on the cyclical nature of organized crime, institutional corruption, and bureaucratic inefficiency. In the context of the series itself, it’s a thought-provoking conclusion about the nature of society and the way things both change and stay the same over time. Pertaining more to television at large, however, The Wire’s resolution proved that major series don’t need to end with clarity or cliffhangers to be compelling.

The air of infamy that surrounded Game of Thrones thanks to its final season has begun to dissipate — the success of House of the Dragon and A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms has been influential in this — but the impact of what the epic fantasy series actually changed is still being felt. This isn’t a reference to the plotting of the story, but rather to the way television as a medium changed in the wake of Game of Thrones’ finale and the outpouring of audience disgust.

It shifted the paradigm of television storytelling. Suddenly, it wasn’t enough to run with an enticing premise and figure it out on the go. Showrunners and writers had to approach major, ongoing series with a mind to how the story would end, or risk facing strong backlash from the fanbase. Granted, some major series haven’t learned the lesson in the ensuing years, and the nature of television storytelling was already evolving due to the rampant rise in streaming services, but Game of Thrones’ conclusion has proven to be a cautionary tale of negligence and subversion, with most shows since opting to conclude in a manner that honors central themes and character dynamics of their respective stories.

Barry is one of those series that has come out since the fallout of Game of Thrones, one where the lessons HBO learned from its failure have been most evident. After four seasons following the violent hitman struggling to transition from a life of crime to a career on the stage, Barry ends with a poignant sense of inevitable tragedy that dismantles the antihero redemption trope, then closes with a 10-year time jump that sees Barry’s (Bill Hader) life being recontextualized and completely misinterpreted in an adoring Hollywood biopic.

The thematic notes that Barry flaunted throughout the four seasons are beautifully handled in “wow,” which also adds a healthy dose of social commentary at the expense of glamorizing entertainment and the audiences that embrace it. The series as a whole is redefined in its final minutes, pivoting from a black comedy crime thriller to a scorching indictment of Hollywood sanitization. With every major character also facing appropriate ends to their arcs, Barry’s finale is a masterclass in how to end a story with thematic punch and emotional deftness.

There have been plenty of series that have completely changed their own nature in their finale; some have even altered the fabric of television with their resolutions, but few have done what Curb Your Enthusiasm did with its swan song outing. The hit comedy series follows the sensationalized life of Larry David, the producer of Seinfeld, as he drifts through Hollywood aimlessly. After 12 seasons and 120 episodes, it ends with Larry on trial in Atlanta, with “No Lessons Learned” delivering a meta recreation of Seinfeld’s infamous and divisive finale.

It’s clever, endearing, and cathartic for all who felt undermined by Seinfeld’s finale, especially as it ends on a twist that sees Larry walk free, leading him and Jerry Seinfeld to declare that would have been a better conclusion for their hit ‘90s sitcom. It’s an admission that Seinfeld was flawed that also serves as a perfect finale for Curb Your Enthusiasm in its own right, making it possibly the only TV show ever made with a finale that has directly changed the perception of a separate series.

Criminally underrated, Show Me a Hero is a breathtaking political drama that uses the tragic true story of the youngest mayor of Yonkers, New York, to explore the complexities of desegregation, the cost of political conviction, and how grassroots humanity clashes violently with institutional power. Oscar Isaac is brilliant as Nick Wasicsko, with much of the miniseries revolving around his desire to embrace a low-income housing project that many of his constituents are viciously opposed to.

The miniseries adamantly rejects the comfort of white savior heroics, delving into the grim reality of the true story that sees Wasicsko grow paranoid after losing an election and eventually commit suicide. It shatters the tone of an idealistic, progressive politician navigating the red tape of city hall, highlighting Wasicsko’s egotistical knack for defining his self-worth through his career as a damning fault, while also illustrating the callous, arduous nature of true systemic change.

The Leftovers is one of those series that feels like everything changes every episode. While it is a slow and meditative show, the depth it invests in its characters constantly brings new revelations to the fold, not only in their own lives as they navigate heartache, frustration, and rage, but in accordance with overarching story themes of grief, trauma, and the human condition as well. Overall, The Leftovers unfolds in a world reeling from the “Sudden Departure”, an inexplicable event in which two percent of the world’s population vanished in an instant.

The series finale, “The Book of Nora,” transpires years after the rest of the series, exploring an uneasy reunion between Nora (Carrie Coon) and Kevin (Justin Theroux). It revels in the surrealist intensity and compassionate dramatic might that defined the series at its best, ending on an unproven explanation as to what caused the Sudden Departure, then leans on empathy and choice in its final moments, rather than a traditional, plot-centered resolution. It’s a bold decision, but it perfectly suits the series’ overarching tone and brings touching closure to Kevin and Nora’s relationship.

Blending corporate satire with engrossing, high-stakes drama, Succession fast became one of HBO’s greatest ever series in its exploration of the vying for power that erupts between three siblings when their father, the aging founder and head of a multi-billion-dollar multimedia company, signals his intent to retire. It is a manic fight for power and position with no blow being deemed below the belt, and yet the fourth and final season still ends in a manner that leaves viewers numb and shocked.

The kinship the Roy siblings display as they stand against Lukas Matsson’s (Alexander Skarsgård) efforts to buy Waystar RoyCo gives way to total desperation and betrayal. It proves that the inherited wealth and influence the Roys flaunted made them incapable of ever following in their father’s footsteps, and their festering animosity with one another, as their shared childhood trauma went unresolved, was instrumental in them fragmenting and losing everything they wanted. It is quite brilliant how a series that features such unlikable and conniving characters leaves the audience feeling hollow, if not completely rattled, at their failure.

Based on Gillian Flynn’s novel of the same name, Sharp Objects is a methodical, meandering crime thriller that anchors its drama in Camille Preaker’s (Amy Adams) investigation of the murder of two young girls and the inner turmoil she experiences as she returns to her hometown of Wind Gap and is confronted with the ghosts of her traumatic childhood. It holds a captivating Southern Gothic atmosphere, a litany of exceptional performances, and a macabre balance of character drama and murder mystery, but its greatest asset is the festering sense of dreadful unpredictability it exudes throughout the entirety of its eight-episode run.

Even with viewers accustomed to its volatile plot twists, Sharp Objects completely changes the nature of its story in its final episode. In fact, it does so many times. The most striking of which comes in the final minute of the series, revealing a horrifying truth that shatters Camille’s sense of self-growth and safety. Accompanied by a credits sequence intercut with shots that force the audience to bask in the terrifying truth, Sharp Objects delivers an unforgettable ending that elevates it from a gripping psychological thriller to a masterclass in nightmarish suspense.

Six Feet Under is essentially a tale of mortality and the human condition wrapped up in a dark comedy about the dysfunctional Fisher family and their management of a funeral home in the aftermath of their patriarch’s death. It’s funny, often poignant and sad, always thought-provoking and compelling, and it ends with what is arguably the greatest series finale in the history of television, a feat of storytelling steeped in catharsis, closure, and beautiful thematic touch.

Within the context of the series itself, it recalibrated all the death and drama as being a simple fact within the wonder of life, transforming the show from being an acidic satirical dramedy to a powerful ode to family and existence itself. Its flash-forward montage and evocative use of Sia’s “Breath Me” only add to the emotional resolution. Through this, Six Feet Under even redefined the commercial possibilities for television, with the episode marking a major breakthrough for Sia that proved a perfect needle-drop moment could define a series’ legacy for decades to come.

It should come as no surprise that The Sopranos’ finale altered the course of television history. After all, the series itself did so from its very first episode. Whether it was through presenting such a complex and challenging antihero in Tony Soprano (James Gandolfini), pioneering long-form serialized drama on the small screen, or simply through its stronger appetite for graphic material, The Sopranos revolutionized the modern age of television drama, and its finale marks another major evolutionary step for storytelling in the medium.

It is iconic in its own right. The diner, the uneasy tension, Journey’s “Don’t Stop Believin’”, and, of course, the cut to black. It was divisive at the time, but it has come to be heralded as a stroke of genius. Almost 20 years on, people still discuss what they think happened to Tony, but from the perspective of television drama, it proved that series didn’t necessarily need to end with neat, conclusive closure to be rewarding. It proved ambiguity had a huge part to play in 21st-century television; it redefined how audiences engaged with television (especially as it coincided with the rising popularity of the internet and social media), and it remains one of the best and boldest TV finales of all time.

Elden Ring: The Convergence Feels Like An Entirely New Game

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The arrival of Elden Ring on the scene really was a stunning next step for FromSoftware, who had already blown people away so many times with releases like the Dark Souls series and Bloodborne. The sprawling open-world RPG really is a spectacular example of the genre, and it provides players with hundreds of hours of entertainment in its own right.

That said, the game did come out all the way back in 2022, and many of its biggest fans and players have already explored pretty much everything it has to offer. That's where the game's modding community comes into play, and they have continually delivered new and worthwhile experiences that breathe new life into the base experience.

One of those in particular goes above and beyond to bring the community new content, and it's well worth considering if you're planning to embark on a fresh playthrough soon.

Over on Nexus Mods, you should be able to find the download page for The Convergence, an exhaustive overhaul of Elden Ring that adds new weapons, enemies, spells, bosses, areas of the map, and just about anything else you can think of.

Happily, the developer has published a full list of features included in the project in a broad sense, which are as follows:

The class-based progression system is undoubtedly going to be the one that makes the biggest tangible difference to how you play the game. What it will provide is a thematic new purpose that allows players to grow into their character in a unique way, also increasing replayability if you start a fresh game with another class, thanks to the unique starting location and progression paths.

Equally, going out into the world and being able to take on new bosses is a great way to extend the experience even further. With the new equipment in your hands, you can also find other ways to dispatch them as quickly as possible, and the additional teleportation options are a great quality-of-life addition for the gamer who is short on spare time.

As with all the downloads over on Nexus Mods, The Convergence is entirely free to download for your next Elden Ring game. It is currently only available on PC, with a port over to other platforms unlikely to emerge in the future.

Manchester United want a genuine superstar to headline the Mauricio Pochettino era at Old Trafford

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Manchester United have identified Nico Paz as the successor to Bruno Fernandes and the man to build their new team around.

That's according to a new report that puts the Red Devils in the midst of a bidding war for the Argentine superstar, with compatriot Mauricio Pochettino about to take the reins at Old Trafford.

Pochettino is stepping down from his role with the United States to become the eighth man to take the Manchester United job on a permanent basis since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013 – and there could be a huge statement made in the market.

Bruno Fernandes has been arguably the greatest United buy of the post-Ferguson era, but after having the season of his life last term, the 32-year-old has shown his age this season, and FourFourTwo understands that for the first time, the Red Devils are starting to consider life beyond their talismanic captain.

While Pochettino is widely expected to build around the captain when he officially arrives through the door, Fichajes reports that United are one of a handful of European heavyweights vying to sign Como superstar Paz.

The outlet stars that the 22-year-old is seen as “a key figure to lead the team's rebuilding”, adding, “the club views him as a clear choice to replace Bruno Fernandes” – but it certainly won't be easy to lure him, with rivals able to offer more.

Premier League champions Arsenal are named among the suitors as they “see him as the ideal successor to Martin Odegaard”, with the 27-year-old Norwegian out of contract in 2028, while Chelsea are also mentioned in an ambitious plan to combine Paz with Morgan Rogers, Joao Pedro and Cole Palmer.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Paris Saint-Germain are named as the final giant in the quartet to “intensify contact” with Paz and Como in December, though two stumbling blocks remain for all four in their bid to secure the playmaker.

When the Northern Italian outfit purchased Paz from Real Madrid, Los Blancos inserted an €80 million buy-back clause – and while Arda Guler and Jude Bellingham are currently shining at the Bernabeu, they also have a 50 per cent sell-on clause for any future deal.

This could drive the price up for Paz – along with any potential bidding war – with Como keen on receiving a £100m fee for a player they believe to be a generational talent.

United's pitch to Paz will surely centre on something that none of the other suitors can offer, however, in making him the face of their new project.

While Pochettino could well draw on coaching a fellow Argentine and note his excellent work in the past with the likes of Dele Alli and Cole Palmer at No.10, United have the space to allow Paz to flourish – if he is willing to take the huge leap to a side who are nowhere near competing for the biggest trophies at this moment in time.

Balogun is worth €50m, according to Transfermarkt. United take on Liverpool when Premier League action resumes after the international break.

Xbox boss announces Wolfenstein 3 and confirms new game in development at idSoftware despite reported job cut "bloodbath"

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"One of gaming's most important franchises".

Xbox boss Asha Sharma has confirmed Wolfenstein 3 is in development at MachineGames, the Swedish studio behind the acclaimed Wolfenstein: The New Order, Wolfenstein 2: The New Colossal, and 2024's celebrated Indiana Jones and the Great Circle. Additionally, she revealed Doom developer idSoftware - one of the Microsoft-owned studios heavily impacted by sweeping Xbox job cuts earlier this year - is now working on a new game.

Sharma has, of course, been on a bit of a PR offensive of late, positioning herself alongside snippets of positive news at a time Xbox is embroiled in a massive business "reset" effort that has resulted in proposed studio closures and mass layoffs that will ultimately see 3,200 Xbox employees lose their jobs. This week's Wolfenstein 3 reveal similarly arrived in the form of a tweet posted directly from her social media account.

"Wolfenstein 3 is officially in production at MachineGames," Sharma wrote on X. "One of gaming's most important franchises, and a team that has done remarkable work bringing it forward. Looking forward to sharing more." As for idSoftware's new project, confirmation of that - again attributed directly to Sharma - appeared in a post on Bethesda's website. "Slaughter Mode comes to Doom: The Dark Ages - Revelations on Monday," she wrote, "and the team is already working on its next game."

In July, sweeping job cuts at Xbox resulted in 96 of idSoftware's 136 employees being laid off in a move one former team member described as a "bloodbath". Since then, idSoftware co-studio director Marty Stratton has attempted to downplay the cuts, recently insisting the studio was "doing great". He added id was "working more closely than ever" with MachineGames - a studio which appears to have escaped Sharma's restructuring efforts so far.

This week's Wolfenstein and idSoftware news follows similar efforts by Xbox to reassure fans their favourite franchises are unaffected by the recent layoffs. Shortly after substantial job cuts at Bethesda were confirmed, the company moved to insist The Elder Scrolls 6 was still "on the way" and that its plans were "unaffected" (the company has since released a trailer announcing its subtitle). It also confirmed a new Fallout game was in development.

Xbox's year of bad news - which includes ongoing boycott efforts relating to Microsoft's controversial involvement with the Israeli military - comes as the division celebrates its 25th anniversary. Earlier this week, Sharma shared a memo to staff marking the occasion, albeit one in which she seemingly championed a gen-AI future for games development, enthusing that, "A small team will be able to build worlds that today demand hundreds of developers".

The Golden Scale in Zelda OoT is a reward and prize from the Fishing Minigame in Lake Hylia

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Golden Scale in Zelda Ocarina of Time is a reward for winning the Fishing minigame off the edge of Lake Hylia, but that's easier said than done. There's several factors involved in the Remake OoT's fishing game, as well as certain elements that have been tweaked since the original. It's worth it though, as the Golden Scale upgrades your ability to dive so you can reach even further depths, including getting the Heart Container Piece from the scientist's lab just outside. If you want to know more, I'll explain what you need to unlock the Golden Scale in Ocarina of Time, and how to beat the fishing game and catch a fish sufficiently large enough to win.

For more useful items, check out where to get the Lenses of Truth in Zelda Ocarina of Time!

The only way to get the Golden Scale in Zelda Ocarina of Time Remake is to go to the Fishing Pond in East Lake Hylia as an adult, and catch a fish of 60cm or larger.

The fishing minigame costs 30 Rupees to play as an adult, but you can keep making fishing attempts until you have gotten a fish big enough, so effectively you need only ever pay once for this reward.

Once you have the fish, make sure you choose to keep it, then bring it over to the Fisherman who runs the pond and he'll give you the Golden Scale as a reward.The Golden Scale allows you to dive deeper than before. Most players will have the Silver Scale at this point, which allows a medium depth dive, acquired while trying to find Ruto in Zelda Ocarina of Time.

Israeli police arrest five in $5M WhatsApp crypto trading probe

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Police suspect the group drew up fictitious loan agreements to disguise crypto trading proceeds it transferred directly into bank accounts.

Israeli police arrested five people on Tuesday in a money laundering investigation involving about 16 million shekels ($5 million) in crypto transactions arranged through a WhatsApp group.

Investigators from the National Unit for Combating Economic Crime within Lahav 433 suspect the group provided crypto services without a license and failed to report transactions to Israel’s tax authorities. Police allege the group used fictitious agreements to make crypto proceeds transferred into bank accounts look like loans, according to The Jerusalem Post.

Authorities seized wallets, computers and tens of thousands of shekels in cash while searching the suspects’ homes. Investigators questioned the five on suspicion of money laundering, fraud and providing financial asset services without a license.

Israel’s Tax Authority announced two other arrests on Oct. 6 in cases involving suspected failures to report crypto activity. One involved trades worth tens of millions of dollars. In the other, an Ashdod resident allegedly failed to report income from about $13 million in crypto trades between 2019 and 2021.

A November 2024 audit by Israel’s State Comptroller found that about 500 people reported crypto activity each year on average from 2018 to 2022, out of at least 200,000 potential filers.

Spain vs England: Nations League prediction, kick-off time, TV, live stream, team news, h2h results, odds

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Standard's journalism is supported by our readers. If you click links to other sites on this page, we will earn commission.

England and Spain both have eyes on top spot in Nations League A3 ahead of a heavyweight showdown this weekend.

The Three Lions guaranteed themselves a top-two finish after beating Croatia on Thursday, but can still topple the reigning world and European champions.

With five matches played apiece, it all comes down to this. England currently trail Spain by three points, which means only a win will see them qualify for the quarter-finals as group winners by virtue of a superior goal difference.

Spain, who have won their last 12 matches in a row, have the luxury of knowing a point is enough to keep them top and thus secure a, potentially, easier knockout phase route.

Spain vs England is scheduled for a 7.45pm GMT kick-off on Sunday, November 15, 2026.

The match will take place at the Metropolitano Stadium, home of La Liga giants Atletico Madrid.

TV channel: In the UK, Spain vs England in the 2026/27 Nations League is being televised live and free to air on ITV, with coverage of the match beginning at 7pm on ITV1.

Live stream: Catch the game live online through the ITVX website and app, which is free with a registration.

Live blog: You can follow all the action with Standard Sport’s live blog, featuring expert insight and analysis from England correspondent Dom Smith.

England could have Ezri Konsa and Djed Spence available for the showdown in the Spanish capital after the defenders missed the win over Croatia through injury.

Thomas Tuchel said in the pre-Croatia press conference that the pair were due to resume team training on Saturday to be ready for the Spain game.

If fit, Konsa and Spence would be options to replace Trent Alexander-Arnold (thus seeing Jarell Quansah move out to right-back) and Lewis Hall respectively, to provide England with greater defensive security.

The front four could be unchanged from the Croatia game with Harry Kane - winning his 127th senior cap - leading the line ahead of Jude Bellingham, Morgan Rogers and Anthony Gordon.

For Spain, the big concern was Lamine Yamal after the Barcelona star sat out the win over the Czech Republic because of a knee issue.

However, Luis de la Fuente revealed afterwards that Yamal is in “perfect condition” to start and play 90 minutes which should see him replace Yeremy Pino in attack.

The reverse fixture at Wembley Stadium in September was nothing short of a classic as England had their moments in matching the world champions.

Ultimately, Spain’s class proved decisive as they ran out 3-2 winners and, in truth, they would have won by a bigger margin had it not been for some wayward finishing.

Spain are simply in their groove and we cannot see England ending their long unbeaten run.

England did win this very fixture on their last visit to Spain in October 2018 when Raheem Sterling (x2) and Marcus Rashford had them 3-0 up before half-time.

Paco Alcacer and Sergio Ramos reduced the deficit in the second half, but the Three Lions held on for a famous win.

Nagarro publica resultados no auditados del tercer trimestre de 2026

Manchester United have confirmed the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino as the club’s new head coach.

Pochettino arrives following the decision to sack Michael Carrick on Thursday and will be in place for United’s next Premier League fixture away to Liverpool on November 22.

The Argentine is currently the head coach of the United States national team and will join United after their two Concacaf Nations League quarter-final fixtures against Haiti.

A statement from United on Friday read: ‘Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become the Manchester United men’s head coach from 18 November.

‘The club has reached an agreement with the U.S. Soccer Federation for Mauricio and his staff to be released after the conclusion of their responsibilities with the U.S. Men’s National Team.

‘AI and Bitcoin Are on One Big Trade Now,’ Says Anthony Pompliano

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As Bitcoin’s price continues to fall further, sparking a fresh wave of panic across the broader crypto market, renowned Bitcoin advocate Anthony Pompliano has issued a statement to investors.

In a recent X post published by the pro-Bitcoin advocate on November 13, Pompliano urged investors to remain calm even as Bitcoin revisited $67,000 for the first time since August.

While Pompliano acknowledged the current market weakness, pointing out that Bitcoin was falling to price levels last seen just three months ago, he urged Bitcoin investors to remain calm.

While addressing Bitcoin's price decline, Pompliano related the situation to AI, another fast-growing technology, drawing attention to the growing relationship between the two sectors.

Pompliano considers the ongoing market movements as evidence that both Bitcoin and AI have become part of one major investment trade.

Although he did not explicitly explain how Bitcoin and AI have become one big trade, it appears that Pompliano is raising awareness of how closely investors may now view the two major investment products in the technology and financial markets.

As such, Pompliano is urging investors to remain positive about Bitcoin’s long-term potential, suggesting that the same market forces driving interest in AI could also influence a potential recovery for Bitcoin.

Pompliano’s comment has come as Bitcoin faces renewed selling pressure, retesting its three-month low at around $67,000 after declining over 5% in the last 24 hours.

Bitcoin’s current price movements have raised questions about whether the decline signals a deeper market correction or simply another temporary setback, causing commentators to disagree with Pompliano’s call for confidence.

This weekend will mark a return to the club’s bread and butter, with Plymouth due up on the blue side of the Steel City.

Owls legend Chris Waddle has warned Challinor must “get to grips” with his players in order to reingite Wednesday’s promotion push.

He said: “He needs to get to grips with the players in his squad and their best roles on the grass and he needs to do it very, very quickly. The trouble with Sheffield Wednesday is that he's got players who don't actually play in their proper positions, apart from the back four.

“In midfield, do you play Barry Bannan as a six, an eight, or a 10? You have other players who just seem to run around. He's got the nucleus of a squad if he can get the balance right. That's what's letting Sheffield Wednesday down - the shape of the team.”

Here are the key TV and streaming details ahead of Wednesday’s clash with Plymouth.

Kick-off at Hillsborough is scheduled for 3pm tomorrow (Saturday, November 14).

Yes - the match will be shown live on TV. It has not been given top billing on any of the main Sky Sports channels but can be viewed as an alternative broadcast via the Sky Sports+ service. Subscriptions can be purchased online.

The 3pm blackout can only be enforced when half of the matches across a country’s top two tiers are played at the same time and there are none scheduled to take place.

Supporters can stream the action via the official Sky Sports app or on NOW TV with a valid subscription. There will also be live audio commentary on BBC Radio Sheffield.

Manchester United: Mauricio Pochettino appointed manager on two-and-a-half year deal

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Manchester United have confirmed the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino as head coach, as he replaces Michael Carrick.

Pochettino has signed a two-and-a-half year deal at Old Trafford, marking his fourth stint in English football following spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea.

The Argentine succeeds Carrick - who was sacked on Thursday - after overseeing a disappointing start to the Premier League season, with Man United sitting in the relegation zone after winning just one of their opening 10 matches.

Pochettino, however, will not start work as Man Utd boss straight away as he is set to continue in his role as USMNT manager for their CONCACAF Nations League two-legged quarter-final against Haiti.

That said, the appointment does mean Pochettino will be in the dugout at Anfield next Sunday to take charge of his first Man United game against bitter rivals Liverpool.

“Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become the Manchester United men’s head coach from 18 November,” a statement reads.

“The club has reached an agreement with the U.S. Soccer Federation for Mauricio and his staff to be released after the conclusion of their responsibilities with the U.S. Men’s National Team.

“The Argentinian is renowned for his high-intensity, attacking style of football, his dedication to building strong, cohesive teams and his outstanding record of developing individual players.

“Pochettino has consistently performed at the highest level of football across multiple leagues. He began his coaching career with Espanyol in 2009 before joining Southampton and achieving a club-record Premier League points tally in 2014.

“During five full seasons at Tottenham Hotspur, Pochettino established the club as regular UEFA Champions League contenders, securing qualification in four consecutive seasons and leading the club to its first final in the competition in 2019.

How Ferguson-inspired Pochettino can succeed where Carrick failed at Man Utd

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Maurucio Pochettino (right) was first mentioned as a contender to manage Manchester United when Louis van Gaal was sacked in May 2016

When Mauricio Pochettino was asked the three Premier League managers he would have loved to have played for, he was unequivocal.

"Sir Alex Ferguson, Sir Alex Ferguson, Sir Alex Ferguson," replied Pochettino, who on Friday was named Manchester United manager.

In 2023, the Argentine - then at Chelsea, and dressed in full Blues training gear - was being interviewed by Sky Sports. Still he felt a need to express his adoration for Ferguson.

Six years prior to that interview, while managing Tottenham, he had shared lunch with the legendary former United manager.

"When I was young, when I started my career as a manager, he was always my inspiration, my reference," Pochettino said.

"And when you can share two hours with a person who was the greatest manager in the history of football, you can only enjoy."

Hiring United managers has been complicated since Ferguson's departure in 2013, and one of the most prominent complications has been a back-and-forth around the club's DNA.

For some, winning by any means necessary should take precedence, while others believe the style of play and cultural heritage of United's most successful period is vital to the club's identity.

In recent years, United's managerial merry-go-round has alternated between European coaches with their own fixed styles of play, and former players from the Ferguson era.

But between his tactics of choice and his adoration for the former United boss, Pochettino may be uniquely placed to bridge the gap between both camps.

Pochettino a decade in Man Utd's making - but Carrick deserves better

Michael Carrick's United produced a series of good results to finish last season with a run unmatched by any other team in the league. They looked great, but it was a greatness built on attacking in a transition-heavy manner.

Under Carrick, in games they had less than 50% of the ball, they only lost once. In those 13 games, they boasted a win-rate of 62%. This dropped to 36% in games they had the majority of possession.

This style of play suited their direct attackers and protected their defenders.

At the same time, Casemiro's set-piece prowess provided United a reliable way to break the deadlock against deep defences - something they have struggled with this season.

With United a goal up, opponents stepped forward, giving Carrick's men more space. They could play to their strengths again, attacking quickly. It worked.

But teams at the top are reluctant to play in this way. Carrick's vision for United was a side that played slick football, with short passes in build-up intuitively, referencing Paris St-Germain's midfield three as a source of inspiration.

Building up in this manner, to entice and escape from pressure, before looking to find the forwards quickly, was the plan Carrick was working towards.

This didn't come to fruition and trying to take United from a successful counter-attacking side to one that could reliably beat teams while keeping the ball was the same hurdle that undid Ole Gunnar Solskjar and Erik ten Hag.

Former players under Sir Alex Ferguson, both Ole Gunnar Solskjar and Michael Carrick leaned into Unitedâs historic style when crafting their game-models

Pochettino gives United an interesting solution to this problem.

In his own words, he has described his sides as needing "to be protagonists, always press high, work hard and be dynamic". He refers to himself as a "disciple" of the legendary Argentina boss Marcelo Bielsa, who he played for.

If you wanted an immediate comparison in the Premier League to a coach who resembles Pochettino's ideas in possession, it would be worth looking at Liverpool's Andoni Iraola, who similarly cites Bielsa as his reference.

These are coaches who, while taking the initiative, encourage vertical football. For as different as the clubs are, both the fans of United and Liverpool have enjoyed success when their teams have played high-octane football.

This summer's World Cup was a strong showcase of Pochettino's style of play and his recent tactical adaptation.

Rather than a fixed, positional style of play - akin to the work of somebody like Manchester City's Enzo Maresca - Pochettino's USA had only certain players being tasked with holding their position.

This approach posed problems for teams who defended in a zonal manner - defending specific areas rather than individual players.

The US midfielders being able to move freely meant they could step into areas of the pitch between the zones that were being defended, which resulted in their players often being unmarked with time and space on the ball.

By playing in close proximity to each other in the middle of the pitch, the US found another benefit.

Small-space passing combinations helped draw the opposing team up towards the ball and combined with clever off-ball movement to stretch the pitch, allowed the US to find their striker with few defenders around him.

Pochettino's men looked to thread through-balls in behind and early after enticing the opposition's press and the speedy Folarin Balogun was able to turn those chances into goals.

Here is a good example of USA's fluid midfield four under Pochettino. The right-sided midfielder McKennie has placed himself near his three midfield team-mates, leaving the right half-space empty. With a four against three in that area of the pitch, USA are best placed to play quick combinations before looking to find their attackers in space.

Here we can the swapping of left-winger Pulisic and left-back Robinson. In this example, Robinson inverts into the fluid midfield four with Pulisic positioned on the touchline for balance. When out wide, Pulisic was able to showcase impressive one-against-one dribbling throughout the match too.

This combination of a fluid midfield, small-space passing and direct through-balls doesn't sound dissimilar to what Carrick was trying to move towards implementing this season.

And although that may worry United fans, the similarity in principles will benefit the players.

"My philosophy is to give a freer style with a good organisation," were Pochettino's words in 2014.

Although philosophically similar, that latter part - a clearer organisation to his freer style - is likely to be what Carrick was unable to reliably offer his players.

For fans of top sides, fast-paced, vertical football tends to be accepted when it comes from a place of being proactive - think Ferguson's United or Jurgen Klopp's Liverpool - wanting to keep the ball, but not passing it for passing's sake.

Fast-paced, vertical football tends to be frowned upon when it comes from a place of conceding possession and defending your own goal for long periods of the match.

The first half of Carrick's stint did appear proactive, while his 4-2-4 press worked well. Leaving four players high was a risk that allowed them to win the ball back and score quickly, but as teams figured this out, United failed to adapt.

There also appeared to be a drop in the team's fitness the longer United went from the departure of ex-boss Ruben Amorim, who left United well conditioned, albeit on the back of a schedule without European football.

Here we see United's narrow 4-2-4 defensive shape in the first half against Como

Hiring Pochettino will immediately begin the process of turning United into a well-drilled side, capable of applying intense pressure, likely in a co-ordinated manner.

Pochettino enjoyed relative success with Spurs, built on this intensity and co-ordination, that differentiated him from the rest of the league.

With that unique selling point more common now than a decade ago, how he builds on it will dictate how his tenure unfolds.

But watching Brighton this season, he can be assured that an approach founded on intensity off the ball can work in the Premier League in 2026, with Fabian Hurzeler's men second in the league, with the fourth highest average possession, 10 games in.

Pochettino, from a footballing point of view, feels extremely aligned to the values of Ferguson's United, and as a result will implement numerous similarities to the things Carrick wanted, but ultimately failed, to deliver.

His wealth of experience make him a more proven entity than the departing United manager and if he can get his team fit and pressing high, in typically Pochettino fashion, they should see an upturn in form.

Expanding on his managerial approach, while managing Spurs he explained how "the most important thing [was] to create a good shape for players to be comfortable and to show their real qualities".

Rather than relying on pre-defined pattern, he looks to empower individuals to solve attacking problems. Taking this approach means recruitment of top talent matters more than ever.

"He [gave] me the confidence and licence to go where I wanted on the pitch, where I [could] use my strengths," said Cole Palmer, who enjoyed his most productive Premier League season under Pochettino at Chelsea.

United have good attackers but they will need to take the same initiative Palmer did, if platformed to their strengths - something Pochettino does well.

If they don't, and if Pochettino is to stumble, it will likely be in United's inability to break down stubborn defences when asked to dominate the ball - in what would feel like Groundhog Day, again.
