TCL Electronics (01070.HK) Reports Significant Growth in Results for the First Three Quarters of 2026

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Adjusted Profit Attributable to Owners of the Parent Surges 54.5% Year-on-Year to HK$2.24 Billion

Global Business Continues to Grow, with Strong Operating Performance in International Markets

HONG KONG, Nov. 13, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- TCL Electronics Holdings Limited ("TCL Electronics" or the "Company", 01070.HK) today announced its unaudited results for the first three quarters of 2026, covering the nine months ended 30 September 2026. During the reporting period, the Company firmly implemented its dual-drive strategy of "Globalisation" and "Mid-to-High-End Premiumisation" and actively deepened cooperation with global channels, achieving strong growth in operating performance. For the first three quarters of 2026, the Company recorded total revenue of HK$95.79 billion, representing a year-on-year increase of 15.7%. Gross profit increased by 29.5% year-on-year to HK$16.26 billion, while gross profit margin increased by 1.8 percentage points year-on-year to 17.0%. The overall expense[1] ratio was 11.9%. Driven by both improved operating efficiency and the realisation of economies of scale, adjusted profit attributable to owners of the parent increased by 54.5% year-on-year to HK$2.24 billion.

In terms of strategic development, the Company continued to advance industrial synergies, the integration of core assets and the optimisation of its business structure. On 31 March 2026 (after trading hours), the Company entered into a transaction framework agreement with Sony in relation to the home entertainment business. The two parties proposed to establish a joint venture to jointly build a new global home entertainment ecosystem, providing important strategic support for the expansion into the mid-to-high-end market. In addition, on 15 July 2026 (after trading hours), the Company announced its proposed acquisition of the TCL air-conditioning business for a total consideration of HK$5.61 billion. The transaction was completed on 2 November 2026, and the relevant business has been consolidated into the Company's financial statements since the completion date, laying an important foundation for the Company to comprehensively implement its "All-category Smart Device" strategy. On 24 August 2026, the Company announced that it was considering the spin-off and separate listing of its photovoltaic business through a distribution in specie. The Company will comprehensively and prudently evaluate the various impacts of implementing this proposal from the perspective of maximising the interests of all shareholders. Looking ahead to the full year of 2026, the Company is confident that it will achieve sustained and steady growth through firm execution of its strategies.

Continued Enhancement of Global Brand Strength, with Significant Improvements in Profitability of Mid-to-High-End and Large-Sized TVs in International Markets

Underpinned by continued enhancement of its global brand strength, efficient channel expansion and ongoing product mix optimisation, the Company's Display Business recorded revenue of HK$62.38 billion, representing a year-on-year increase of 19.1%, and gross profit of HK$11.51 billion, representing a year-on-year increase of 38.4%. Gross profit margin increased by 2.6 percentage points year-on-year to 18.5%. Benefitting from the continued increase in the proportion of mid-to-high-end and large-sized TVs, revenue from the TCL TV business increased by 16.7% year-on-year to HK$52.03 billion, while gross profit margin increased by 3.2 percentage points year-on-year to 19.5%.

TCL Mini LED TVs maintained rapid growth for the first three quarters of 2026, with global shipments increasing by 57.9% year-on-year and their share of total shipments rising by 6.1 percentage points year-on-year to 17.5%. In particular, shipments in international markets increased by 94.5% year-on-year, driving a 4.4-percentage-point year-on-year increase in the gross profit margin of the TV business in international markets to 19.0%. The Company continued to expand its global channel footprint and deepen the development of key channels. These efforts helped TCL TV rank among the top three globally in sales revenue, maintain its second-place ranking globally in shipment volume, and retain its position as the world's No. 1 brand in TCL Mini LED TV shipments[2]. TCL X11L SQD-Mini LED TV was named one of TIME magazine's "Best Inventions of 2026" in the United States, making it the only Chinese television product selected in this category and further demonstrating the Company's leading position in premium display technology.

In the Small-and-Medium-Sized Display business, the Company adhered to the strategy of "prioritising efficiency and focusing on key markets" and continued to deepen its presence in key channels in Europe and the United States. Leveraging differentiated products such as NXTPAPER eye-care display technology, the business achieved growth against the market trend, with both revenue and gross profit outperforming the industry as a whole. For the first three quarters of 2026, revenue from the Small-and-Medium-Sized Display business increased by 32.2% year-on-year to HK$9.32 billion.

International Internet Business Contributes to High-Quality Earnings, While Photovoltaic Business Achieves Substantive Progress in the European Market

During the Reporting Period, the Company's Internet Business recorded revenue of HK$2.70 billion, representing a year-on-year increase of 28.2%. Among its businesses, the expansion of the higher-margin international internet business continued to accelerate, with its share of Internet Business revenue increasing by 17.0 percentage points year-on-year. This drove a 4.6-percentage-point year-on-year increase in the overall gross profit margin of the Internet Business to 60.9%, continuing to contribute high-quality earnings. In international markets, leveraging its scale advantages, the Company continued to deepen cooperation with internet giants such as Google, Roku and Netflix. The content application TCL Channel continued to undergo iterative upgrades in terms of content richness, user experience and monetisation efficiency. As at the end of September 2026, TCL Channel had a cumulative user base of 56.99 million.

In the Innovative Business, for the first three quarters of 2026, the Company recorded revenue of HK$30.68 billion, representing a year-on-year increase of 8.5%. Among its businesses, the Photovoltaic Business maintained resilient growth amid industry headwinds, with revenue increasing by 11.8% year-on-year to HK$18.18 billion. The business focused on key countries in Europe in international markets and made substantive progress in advancing integrated energy solutions combining photovoltaics, energy storage and heating, leveraging the brand influence of SunPower.

For the first three quarters of 2026, the Company continued to promote the synergistic development of its All-Category Marketing business and improve the layout of its smart device product portfolio. Revenue increased by 3.3% year-on-year to HK$10.95 billion. Meanwhile, in August 2026, the Company launched TCL AiMe, an AI companion and caregiving robot, further enriching its all-scenario smart ecosystem and opening up new growth opportunities in the emerging consumer electronics sector.

[1] Overall expense includes selling and distribution expenses and administrative expenses.

[2] Source: Omdia, global brand TV and Mini LED TV shipment for the first half of 2026.

TCL Electronics Holdings Limited (01070.HK, incorporated in the Cayman Islands with limited liability) has been listed on the Main Board of The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited since November 1999. Its business scope covers display business, innovative business, and internet business. Guided by the business philosophy of "Strategy Guidance, Innovation Driven, Advanced Manufacturing and Global Operation", TCL Electronics actively embraces transformation and innovation and focuses on breaking into the mid-to high-end global market, and strives for an all-category layout for the "Smart IoT Ecosystem". Dedicated to providing users with all-scenario smart healthy living experiences, TCL Electronics aims to become a leading global intelligent terminal enterprise. TCL Electronics is included in the list of eligible shares for the Shenzhen-Hong Kong Stock Connect. It is a constituent stock of the Hang Seng Stock Connect Hong Kong Index, the Hang Seng Composite LargeCap & MidCap Index, the Hang Seng Composite MidCap Index and the "Hang Seng SCHK Electronics Theme Index". Since 2018, the Company has been awarded an ESG rating of A by Hang Seng Indexes Company for several consecutive years.

For more information, please visit TCL Electronics' investor relations website at http://electronics.tcl.com.

Avatar: Seven Havens producer says the animated sequel series "isn't a kids' show"

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Unlike its predecessor Avatar: The Last Airbender, Seven Havens isn't for a younger audience

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Despite its predecessor Avatar: The Last Airbender famously releasing on Nickelodeon for years and catering to a younger audience, the sequel series Seven Havens apparently isn't made for kids.

During an Avatar: Seven Havens Reddit AMA, one fan asked if the team intended to make the show more accessible to a younger audience, to which executive producer and developer Sehaj Sethi replied, "Personally, I think this show really isn’t a kids show. The themes we cover are quite dark and mature. We always intended for this show to technically be accessible to a younger audience, but also be very interesting to older viewers."

The series, which is set around 225 years after The Last Airbender sequel The Legend of Korra, Seven Havens follows twin sisters Pavi and Nisha as they uncover their Avatar origins and protect the last cities that remain centuries after the infamous apocalypse caused by Korra. But even though the show centers around children Nisha and Pavi, Sethi says "we don’t hold back on making them face truly traumatic experiences."

We can see what Sethi means. The show's themes feel very current and quite upsetting. The sisters have to contend with living in a refuge in a war-torn land hit by sandstorms and tornadoes. In the midst of their already complicated lives, Pavi now has to deal with being the new Avatar when she has been taught all her life to hate Avatars due to Korra's actions.

When the show debuted on Paramount Plus, some fans were not too happy about Korra being vilanised. But the big swing seemed to pay off, as Seven Havens debuted to a perfect Rotten Tomatoes. In our Avatar: Seven Havens season 1 review, we call the animated installment "a strong new entry for the franchise, filled with intriguing plot elements and characters," but in part it is "let down by its animation." Seven Havens has already been renewed for season 2.

Avatar: Seven Havens is streaming now on Paramount Plus. For more, see our Avatar: Seven Havens season 1 ending explained, and keep up with new TV shows on the way.

In my spare time, you can find me binging horror movies or getting lost in a cosy little game on my Switch.

Cardinale: "Vogliamo vincere e servire i tifosi. Con MSE possiamo portare a Milano il meglio dello sport e dell'intrattenimento dal vivo americani"

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

RedBird ha una consolidata esperienza nella creazione e nello sviluppo di aziende nei settori dello sport, dei media e dell'hospitality (Legends Hospitality, On Location, YES Network, EverPass).

La neonata Milan Sports & Entertainment (“MSE”), holding che riunirà gli investimenti di RedBird a Milano nei settori dello sport, delle infrastrutture e dell'intrattenimento, metterà a frutto le competenze di RedBird per costruire una piattaforma il cui valore complessivo, le opportunità commerciali e l'influenza globale vadano ben oltre quelli delle singole realtà che la compongono.

Gerry Cardinale, Founder e Managing Partner di RedBird Capital, ha dichiarato: “Negli ultimi 25 anni abbiamo lavorato al fianco dei rights holder nei settori dello sport e dei media per creare imprese innovative che hanno trasformato i rispettivi settori. Questa evoluzione si fonda su un profondo rispetto per il patrimonio di proprietà intellettuale alla base di queste realtà, su una partnership di lungo periodo con i titolari dei diritti e sull'ambizione di valorizzare appieno il potenziale di queste squadre e leghe, dentro e fuori dal campo. Milan Sports & Entertainment rappresenta il risultato di questo percorso e del lavoro portato avanti da quando, quattro anni fa, abbiamo investito per la prima volta in AC Milan. Diventare proprietari di un club ci ha fatto comprendere ancora più a fondo ciò che crea valore nel lungo periodo: vincere, essere al servizio dei tifosi e investire nelle comunità che danno significato a queste istituzioni. Oggi applichiamo questa esperienza su una scala diversa: non a un solo club, a un solo sport o a un solo impianto, ma a un'intera città e a un portfolio che comprende sport, lifestyle e intrattenimento. Conosco Brett Yormark e lavoro con lui da 25 anni, e non riesco a immaginare un dirigente migliore nel settore dello sport internazionale per contribuire a realizzare questa strategia. Grazie a Brett, possiamo portare a Milano il meglio dello sport e dell'intrattenimento dal vivo americani, contribuendo a scrivere un nuovo capitolo nel percorso di crescita e affermazione internazionale della città. Con il Milan come punto di riferimento, che sarà sempre la forza trainante di tutte le nostre iniziative, intendiamo costruire una piattaforma dello sport e dell'intrattenimento di livello mondiale, capace di plasmare il futuro di Milano e dello sport europeo e di riportarlo ai vertici della scena globale”.

Nvidia CEO steps down. And, Sen. Chris Van Hollen to run for majority leader

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is stepping down following reports of his extramarital affairs, the company's board announced. The company's chief financial officer, Colette Kress, has been appointed interim CEO. The company launched an investigation after allegations surfaced that Huang had an affair with one of its clients, who U.S. intelligence officials concluded had been recruited by China's top spy agency. In an email to Nvidia staff, Huang admitted to cheating on his wife and promised to cooperate with the investigation. Nvidia's share price fell sharply Wednesday after the news of his second affair with an Nvidia executive came to light. Huang was widely considered an important business leader in the U.S. and had close ties to President Trump.

Jensen Huang gives a keynote address at the Nvidia Berlin GPU Technology Conference on Oct. 21, 2026. Sean Gallup/Getty Images hide caption

White House envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner have been busy this week working on a new security framework between Russia and Europe and seeking to end the war in Ukraine. They led peace talks in Abu Dhabi, met with Russian President Vladimir Putin in Moscow and then traveled to Ireland to meet with Ukraine's president, Volodymyr Zelenskyy.

Sen. Chris Van Hollen of Maryland has announced he will challenge New York's Chuck Schumer for the Senate majority leader position. Following recent midterm victories, Van Hollen said voters desired a "change from the status quo." Elected to the Senate in 2016 after more than a decade in the House, the 67-year-old progressive gained national attention last year for lobbying for the release of Kilmar Abrego Garcia after his wrongful deportation to a prison in El Salvador. His announcement comes as progressive activists and some Democrats push for the 75-year-old Schumer's replacement. Schumer has been in the role since 2017 but faced criticism from the Democratic base for failing to confront Trump vigorously during a funding dispute in early 2025.

JPMorgan Buys More Strategy and Bitcoin ETF Despite MSCI Saylor Blow

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

JPMorgan is quietly increasing its Bitcoin exposure while the crypto market is being beaten up. The banking giant raised its Strategy holdings by 36% and added another 1 million shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT).

JPMorgan Bitcoin positioning makes an interesting contrast with the current market panic. Bitcoin has plunged below $70,000, wiping out leveraged bullish bets and dragging major altcoins lower. Meanwhile, Michael Saylor’s Strategy faces another institutional headache after MSCI confirmed its removal from major equity indexes.

Wall Street might be seeing something that the market is missing. JPMorgan’s latest holdings show institutional exposure was growing before the latest sell-off, even as Strategy faces mounting pressure.

Could this be smart money positioning for Bitcoin’s next recovery?

JPMorgan held 848,932 Strategy shares worth $131.3 million at the end of September. That’s a 36% increase from its 624,387 shares reported three months earlier.

Its exposure to BlackRock’s Bitcoin ETF grew even further in dollar terms. The bank reported 11.4 million IBIT shares worth $540 million, compared with 10.4 million shares valued at $355.7 million in June.

The IBIT position increased by 9.6% in share count, while its reported market value jumped 52%. JPMorgan also reported smaller positions in other Bitcoin funds, including Grayscale’s Bitcoin Mini Trust and Bitwise’s Bitcoin ETF.

Together, the Strategy and IBIT positions were worth $671 million at September 30. That’s a sizeable institutional footprint across two different ways of gaining Bitcoin exposure.

However, the timing makes this filing particularly interesting. These holdings paint JPMorgan’s third quarter, ending September 30. JPMorgan was building exposure before Bitcoin’s latest breakdown, leaving investors wondering if the bank still sees value in the correction.

There is another detail worth watching. JPMorgan also disclosed substantial put-option exposure against Strategy and IBIT, alongside call positions. Its derivatives book shows a more complicated positioning strategy than a straightforward bullish bet. Still, the increased share counts show that the bank expanded its underlying holdings.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

JPMorgan’s growing Strategy position comes at an awkward moment for Saylor. MSCI confirmed that Strategy will leave its MSCI USA Index following the November 30 market close. The company is also losing its place in the MSCI World and ACWI benchmarks as the index provider implements new eligibility rules for non-operating companies.

JUST IN: MSCI to officially remove Michael Saylor's Strategy $MSTR from its indexes by the end of this month. pic.twitter.com/h1jBFtVU8M

This decision could create another source of selling pressure. Passive funds tracking affected benchmarks generally need to adjust their holdings when constituents disappear. Strategy previously warned that potential index exclusions could trigger billions of dollars in outflows.

JPMorgan previously estimated that MSCI exclusion alone could result in $2.8 billion in selling pressure. The figure could climb substantially if other major index providers follow the same approach.

Yet JPMorgan increased its Strategy holdings during the quarter leading into this decision. That creates an interesting contradiction: one of Wall Street’s largest banks expanded its exposure while the company was heading toward a major index setback.

Bitcoin slipped below $69,000 this morning, its lowest level since August, while crypto liquidations reached $1.6 billion over 24 hours. More than $1.3 billion came from liquidated long positions, highlighting just how aggressively leveraged traders were caught on the wrong side of the move.

This selling can create opportunities for investors with longer time horizons. When leveraged positions disappear, weaker hands are forced out, leaving more room for institutional accumulation once volatility settles.

The MSCI setback, no doubt, creates a potential disconnect between index-driven selling and longer-term investment demand.

The Love Is Blind Season 11 Reunion Was Awful, But That Seems On Par With The Season

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

The Gilded Age Fans Should Mind Their Business About Marian And Larry

After Watching The Invite As A Divorcee, I Have A Take On The Ending

90 Day Fiancé: The Other Way Just Hit On Andi And Nigel's Biggest Issue, And I Don't See Them Getting Over It

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Love Is Blind once again proves that love is not blind, or maybe shouldn’t be, with another disastrous season. However, this may be one of the show’s most unsuccessful seasons in terms of marriages and long-term relationships. The one marriage from the season didn’t even last a year.

An interesting reunion can usually redeem a less-than-exciting season of Love is Blind, which you can watch with a Netflix subscription. We have seen many do just that. However, this reunion confirmed just how disappointing Season 11 is. It magnified Love is Blind’s issues.

Warning: Love is Blind Season 11 reunion spoilers are ahead. Proceed with caution.

I have watched all 11 seasons of Love Is Blind, and the reunions are getting worse. These are supposed to be an opportunity to dig into some of the biggest unanswered questions. They could be a chance to hold people accountable for questionable behavior from previous episodes.

Hosts Nick and Vanessa Lachey achieve some of these things, but not fully. Rob Feeley had one of the season’s biggest moments. It led to many online discussions about his and his friends’ problematic behavior. The reunion barely addresses it.

Other things aren’t fully addressed, including details of some of the breakups. For example, we’re left confused about Michaella Giorgio and Matt Waldrip’s failed relationship. We leave the season with a lot more questions than answers.

The whole point of the reunion is to get answers and closure for the viewers. Even the messiest seasons usually have some bright spots, but Love Is Blind: Boston doesn’t seem to have any. This makes us worry even more about the series’ future.

The reunion had bromance montages, an engagement announcement from someone who was barely on the season, a focus on some men who were barely on the show, a look at Blake Carver’s life in the sea, and a musical number. It seemed like the Love Is Blind Season 11 reunion wanted to do everything but focus on what viewers care about: information about the couples.

I know the show wanted to balance some lighthearted moments with the drama and heaviness. However, they just felt out of place. It took away from the really interesting parts of the reunion. Additionally, it felt extremely ironic that a show that’s supposed to focus on proving love can work under extreme circumstances chose to have “Stop the Wedding!” by Ashe as the season’s theme song.

It further emphasizes how much Love Is Blind doesn’t take itself seriously anymore. We don’t know if the producers still care at all about having successful couples.

Automatically, the Love Is Blind reunion felt uncomfortable. It just became increasingly that way as it progressed. It was an intense season, but the producers could have done more to make the tension less intense. It was either too intense or ridiculous with showing unnecessary things like montages.

Love Is Blind has had plenty of iconic moments, so the show has the capability for that. However, it’s losing its way in producing a well-executed, compelling show about relationships. The season as a whole and the reunion highlights this problem. Hopefully, they can start to pull it back to its old glory in the upcoming season.

We still think Love Is Blind, but it could use some help.

I have been a life long lover of pop culture, especially television, turned that passion into writing about all things entertainment related. When I'm not writing about pop culture, I can be found channeling Gordon Ramsay by kicking people out the kitchen.

Senate Banking Committee asks Jensen Huang and Howard Lutnick to testify over ‘significant national security concerns’ next month, or face subpoenas

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang and Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick have been asked to appear before the U.S. Senate Banking Committee next month to address national security concerns. Senator Tim Scott (R-SC), who leads the committee, confirmed overnight that Huang and Lutnick have been asked to appear on December 3.“Recent reports raise serious concerns about our national security and the protection of sensitive U.S. technology,” he said. “The American people deserve answers.”

Senator Scott’s letter added a weighty conclusion: If either fails to appear before the committee voluntarily, it is “prepared to use all tools at our disposal, including subpoenas, to conduct this legitimate and necessary oversight.”

The Banking Committee hearing is the latest in a series of inquiries into the conduct of Jensen Huang, the former CEO of chipmaking giant Nvidia. Huang resigned from the company he’s spent the past 30 years building after admitting to an extramarital affair with a woman believed to be a Chinese spy.

Reports have since surfaced that Huang had an affair with Nvidia executive Kimberly Powell. Reuters reported yesterday that the entrepreneur had a second affair with a member of staff, quoting unnamed sources.

Nvidia’s CFO, Colette Kress, has been named interim CEO following his resignation.

President Trump told reporters earlier this week that the FBI is investigating the affair between Huang and Alice Huang (no relation), the alleged Chinese spy. China has denied its involvement. Nvidia’s board has also said it is conducting its own independent investigation and has hired Joon H. Kim, former acting U.S. Attorney for the Southern District of New York at Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz, to lead the review.

Senator Scott laid bare the Senate Banking Committee’s authority to request both Huang and Lutnick, a member of the president’s cabinet, to appear before it. His letter to Huang says the committee has “jurisdiction over matters relating to ‘export controls,’ including their application to NVIDIA’s exports of advanced semiconductors.”

The committee is “seeking answers to questions that raise grave national security concerns,” it added in a post on X.

Chairman @SenatorTimScott has requested that NVIDIA CEO Jensen Huang appear on December 3 and Commerce Secretary Lutnick on December 10.The Committee oversees advanced semiconductor export controls and is seeking answers to questions that raise grave national security concerns. https://t.co/HpSxJH213J

Members of the committee have suggested Lutnick had allowed loopholes to persist that allowed high-end chips to continue to be sold to China.

Senator Jim Banks (R-IN) told Fox News last night that he believed Lutnick had “defied President Trump’s directive not to sell these high-end chips to China or to allow them to be sold to Chinese customers, that ended up happening anyway.”

“Secretary Howard Lutnick and Under Secretary Jeffrey Kessler must explain why they have not closed export control loopholes and act today to ensure our advanced technology is not fueling China’s military,” added Senator Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), currently the ranking member on the committee.

Huang is also likely to face disbelief from Senator John Kennedy (R-LA). Senator Kennedy told CNN in an interview last night: “I’m not naive; I’m not Bambi’s baby brother. I know people have affairs; I wouldn’t recommend it, but if you do, you ought to make sure it’s not with a Chinese spy. I mean, check their LinkedIn page for God’s sake.”

Senator Kennedy referred to the debacle as an “intergalactic freak show,” adding: Here’s a man, head of one of the most powerful companies in the world, more than anybody else in the private sector; he’s been making AI policy for America, and certainly with respect to our relationship with China. And then we learn about this.”

U.S. seizes $1.2 billion in cryptocurrency stolen from Binance

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

U.S. authorities have seized around $1.2 billion in virtual assets stolen from cryptocurrency exchange Binance last month, the Department of Justice said Thursday, as it vowed action to tackle cyberscams originating from North Korea.

Over $6 billion was stolen from industry giant Binance on Oct. 29, which the DOJ attributed to actors linked to the North Korean military.

Collective efforts between the U.S. government and private sector have resulted in more than $2.7 billion of that amount being frozen.

The FBI blames the attack, which shook confidence in crypto market security, on members of the North Korean military it refers to as "TraderTraitor."

The DOJ said it was taking coordinated action against what it called the North Korean government's "schemes to fund its regime through the massive theft of cryptocurrency."

That includes "the court-authorized seizure and restraint of more than $1.2 billion worth of cryptocurrency recently stolen from Binance," it said.

DOJ units, law enforcement partners and virtual asset service providers have been working to intercept the stolen assets.

But efforts to track and freeze the assets are ongoing, as TraderTraitor actors continue to launder them through various cryptocurrency channels.

Authorities have also seized cloud-based infrastructure used in a massive identity impersonation scam known as "FaceStealer," which has seen more than $400 million worth of cryptocurrency stolen over 18 months, the DOJ said.

The scheme sees hackers set up fake video meetings using stolen accounts and recordings of people known to the target by using AI-generated deepfakes. The hackers then say a technical problem requires a fix, install malware on a computer and take over the victim's communications, steal their cryptocurrency and target their contacts — sometimes months or more than a year after the initial compromise.

Individuals working in the cryptocurrency and web3 industries are prime targets.

Assistant Director in Charge Patrick Grandy, of the FBI's Los Angeles Field Office, called FaceStealer "a highly effective social engineering scheme capable of deceiving even the most experienced technical and security conscious computer user."

"It exploits our trust in ubiquitous video conference platforms and valued personal relationships. If you can't imagine a scenario where you might fall prey to it, you are not thinking creatively enough," Grandy added.

"North Korea's government bankrolls its rogue regime and illicit weapons program through cryptocurrency theft," Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor said in a statement.

"America will fight back against that corrupt regime's effort to profit through cybercrime. The National Security Division will use every tool at its disposal to seize and return North Korea's ill-gotten gains."

I hate how most smartwatches look, but I'll happily wear the Withings ScanWatch Light every day and with any outfit

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you’re after a beautiful smartwatch with all the basic fitness and health features you need, and which won’t ruin your outfit, look no further than the Withings ScanWatch Light 2. This elegant watch is pricey given its basic functionality, but still offers strong battery life and lots of insight via the companion app. If it’s bang for your buck you’re after, rivals offer more, but none can match the ScanWatch Light’s sheer pizzazz.

Auto activity detection didn’t work for me

Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.

I hate most fitness trackers. Although I love what they do, I really don’t love how they look. For ages now, I’ve been pining for a smartwatch that does all the stuff I need it to when I’m at the gym or on a walk, yet looks like a normal, classic watch. I know, I know, you can set analog watch faces on many smartwatches, but we all know that’s not the same.

Enter the Withings ScanWatch Light. This thing is beautiful. It looks like a classic watch and fits in with even my smartest outfits. And it does all the basic fitness-y things I need it to, although not much more — the “Light” part really does mean light. This is not a feature-packed smartwatch, and certainly isn’t designed for athletes. Instead, it’s a smartwatch for people who want core health features without sacrificing style or ruining the cohesiveness of their outfits.

Find out whether this is the best fitness tracker for you in my full Withings ScanWatch Light review.

White/gray; rose gold/white; black/silver

The Withings ScanWatch Light costs $249 / £229 at Amazon. If you buy direct from Withings, you can choose a metal or leather strap for an extra $49, or a fabric strap for $39.

It certainly isn’t cheap, but look: Withings gear is always pricey. I don’t mind something being expensive if it can back up the price tag somehow. And most Withings products do so by combining charming design, solid build quality, and decent core functionality.

On the first two counts, the ScanWatch Light ticks the boxes. However, this is a stripped-back watch, so price versus functionality is pretty poor. You’re going to get more features for the money with the $249 Apple Watch SE 3. Or the $169 Amazfit Active Premium 3. Or heck, even the $79 Amazfit Bip 6. None match the ScanWatch Light’s elegant looks, though, so my question is: how much is style worth to you?

If you have the extra cash and want similar styling but more features, check out the ScanWatch Light’s bigger siblings: the $369 Withings ScanWatch 2 or $599 ScanWatch Nova.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

In my opinion, the best thing about the ScanWatch Light is its design. I really appreciate the huge array of fitness features of smartwatches like my Amazfit T-Rex 3 Pro. But I much prefer wearing a traditional analog watch when I’m not working out — giant AMOLED slabs and ugly silicone straps don’t really go well with my daily outfits. So, up to now I’ve switched between my analog watch and smartwatch when going to/from the gym, which has been a pain.

The ScanWatch Light is the perfect solution. It looks just like a classic analog watch: simple, elegant, beautiful. The face measures 1.4 inches, which fits my wrist nicely without feeling too bulky.

The watch comes in three different colorways (white/gray, rose gold/white, and black/silver). I chose the black model and love its effortless style and sophistication. It pairs well with all my outfits, from casual daily and smart casual fits up to suits and even my black tux. And the silver bezel and accents pair nicely with the silver and white gold rings I wear on my left hand. Obviously, though, the ScanWatch Light is also a fitness tracker! Which means I have a single watch for both gym, work, and leisure. I never have to switch again.

The standard strap is fluoroelastomer (a type of rubber), so it doesn’t absorb sweat or go gross when working out, and doesn’t chafe either. That said, while I usually dislike the look of rubber/silicone, the ScanWatch Light’s strap is finished with a matte effect, meaning it doesn’t look tacky like my (much cheaper) Amazfit Active. Again, you’re getting the best of both worlds.

I found the strap either a little snug or slightly too loose. In other words, I’m between sizes. This depends entirely on the individual anatomy/width of your wrist, though, so I’m not moaning. And besides, it still wasn’t uncomfortable at all, either when exercising or wearing all day — helped by the ScanWatch Light’s low 0.9-ounce kerb weight.

The buckle is dual-sided, meaning it also serves as a fixed loop to hold the strap tail in place, and there’s a sliding free loop for a second securement point. It’s a solid system and keeps the strap tail from flapping around. It can be a bit fiddly getting the strap through the second buckle opening, though.

As I’d expect from a Withings product, the ScanWatch Light is built extremely well and feels premium. The case is made from stainless steel and the face is covered by Gorilla Glass (the kind used on smartphone screens) so should stand up to daily wear and tear without scratching. It’s waterproof down to 5ATM (five atmospheres) of pressure, so you can take it swimming.

The digital grayscale OLED display is very small, but deliberately so — this is designed to be as minimalist a smartwatch as possible. When you press the digital crown, the watch hands move out of the way of the screen automatically if they need to, which I love. When it’s not in use, the display stays off and thereby blends in nicely with the black watchface, thus not spoiling the analog aesthetic. On the lighter models, though, the black display is always visible.

You control the watch entirely via the steel digital crown. Navigating the menu is fairly clunky, especially when you want to go back through menus. However, the menu system and features aren’t complex to begin with, so it’s fine. You don’t need to use the crown to set the time or date, as it’s all done digitally by pairing the watch to your phone and the Withings app.

As I mentioned up top, this watch bears the “Light” moniker for a reason. If you’re after highly detailed tracking and plenty of on-wrist control/info, the ScanWatch Light isn’t for you.

Don’t get me wrong, there’s a decent array of activities on offer. All the basics are there, of course: running, cycling, rowing, etc. There are indoor versions for treadmills and exercise bikes, as well as some specific activities like badminton, muay thai and more. There’s even a dog walking activity — I’m not sure how much that differs from ordinary walking.

None of the activity modes I used were particularly in-depth, though. Weight lifting, for example, had no interval functionality. They all seem to just measure BPM as you work out. Still, that’s all I really need. (And to be honest, I find the interval weight lifting function on my Amazfit watches a bit fiddly anyway.) You have no real control over the activities on-watch either — you simply use the digital crown to pause and then either resume or stop activities. Nor do you get any detailed info on the watch itself as to your performance or progress.

If you’re a serious fitness enthusiast and want something for hardcore training, you’ll want more in-depth measurements and onboard controls. Check out the Apple Watch Series 12, Withings ScanWatch 2 or Amazfit Active Premium 3. Or the Apple Watch Ultra 3 if budget is no constraint.

What the ScanWatch Light measured was pretty accurate. I compared the BPM analysis and calorific burn to several different machines at my gym and got essentially the same results each time, usually within just a couple of BPM and a couple of dozen calories. The ScanWatch did tell me I had mild bradycardia at first, measuring 52BPM resting. Look, I’m not in terrible shape, but that’s athlete levels, and I was highly stressed at the time due to a bereavement. So go figure. Since then, though, BPM reads have stayed more realistic and consistent. There’s also a VO2 max assessment available via the app — arguably the most important metric to track, generally speaking.

Unfortunately, the ScanWatch Light’s automatic activity detection simply doesn’t work on my test sample. Anything I did at the gym just went completely undetected. I cycled hard for 8 minutes. I ran for 10 minutes. Nothing, until I started the activities manually. I thought it might use GPS movement to detect activities, but when I walked or jogged outside, nothing happened. Weirder yet, on two occasions I've woken up in the night to find the watch has started detecting a run while on my bedside table.

I’ve reached out to Withings to find out why this may be and whether it’s an isolated incident on my test sample. This isn’t a gamechanger for me — I often find automatic detection results in false starts on my Amazfit Active and T-Rex 3 Pro, so I typically disable it anyway. But if a product has a feature, I still expect it to work.

The ScanWatch Light doesn’t have built-in GPS, instead relying on your phone for location services. This means you’ll need to keep your phone on you when you exercise if you want to view routes.

Health tracking-wise, it’s a similar story. The ScanWatch Light doesn’t track much. It’ll measure your BPM as I mentioned above, but there’s no ECG, blood oxygen, or body temperature tracking — you’ll have to upgrade to the pricier ScanWatch 2 or ScanWatch Nova for those.

Aside from BPM, the ScanWatch Light can track your sleep quality and give you an assessment of your breathing quality during sleep — both found on the pricier ScanWatch 2. There’s also menstrual cycle tracking. It’s more of an app feature, but you can add cycle tracking to the watch menu to input your stages. As a dude, I obviously didn’t use this, but it’s still a fundamental feature I expect to see in a fitness tracker.

There’s also a relaxation mode, which is pretty cool. You set a time interval, then how many breaths per minute you want to take. The watch then vibrates to prompt your breaths in and out. It’s a cool little feature for quickly taking a wellbeing break. And there are standing prompts when the watch detects you’ve not stood for a while — all pretty par for the course.

The Withings app is usually one of our favorites. It allows you to customize the ScanWatch Light’s menus and offers lots of information and data based on your measurements, inputs and watch readings. You can track your activities, progress and health data, with lots of graphs for visualization. The app provides a strong basic level of insight for all of this, explaining what terms mean and often linking out to informative articles for detailed explanation. As you can see in the screenshot below, I've tapped through from my BPM graph, to a basic explanation and second chart, and again through to a full detailed heart rate explainer article.

Via the Withings app, all your health data can be synced from centralized apps, like Apple Health, Google Health, or Samsung Health. Withings also partners with major tracking apps like Strava and MyFitnessPal, although if you’re using an iPhone like me, all this is routed through Apple Health to begin with anyway, so I didn’t need to set up any specific connections.

After one week with constant use and workouts 4-5 times per week, I still had 33% of battery left. So you can reasonably expect around 10 days with normal usage. A full charge takes around 2 hours on average, and charging via a 15W charger from 30% took me around an hour and 15 minutes, which tracks with Withings’ claims.

I’m not a fan of the ScanWatch Light’s charging dock. It’s just an awkwardly long piece of thin, bent plastic with an ostensibly meaningless lip on one side. It doesn’t sit particularly well on my bedside table, looks ugly, and struggles to stay in position. Great.

I much prefer the small, unobtrusive dock of my T-Rex 3 Pro, and Apple’s sleek little magnetic dock. But in fairness to Withings, I resent using all docks equally as much — they’re just a sad reality given that sticking a USB-C port on a watch would seriously compromise pressure- and waterproofing.

Look, the Withings ScanWatch Light is overpriced and under-featured for the money. I can admit that. It’s not for athletes or serious fitness enthusiasts. But it isn’t meant for those people. It’s for people like me who want a stylish all-round watch that can track all the basics for general day-to-day fitness and wellbeing without getting too serious. And importantly, for people who don’t want an ugly square hunk of AMOLED display on their wrist, clashing with their outfit.

It’s also built beautifully, has solid battery life and offers lots of visualizations and information via the Withings app. And did I mention it looks fantastic? I know the ScanWatch Light isn’t perfect, but damn, do I love it.

Peter is a Senior Editor at Tom's Guide, heading up the site's Reviews team and Cameras section. As a writer, he covers topics including tech, photography, gaming, hardware, motoring and food & drink. Outside of work, he's an avid photographer, specialising in architectural and portrait photography. When he's not snapping away on his beloved Fujifilm camera, he can usually be found telling everyone about his greyhounds, riding his motorcycle, squeezing as many FPS as possible out of PC games, and perfecting his espresso shots.

Rocket Report: Starship set for historic test flight; a century of Electron

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“You can simplify it as a big tank feeding engines.”

Welcome to Edition 9.19 of the Rocket Report! Will something historic happen this weekend? No orbital rocket has ever been fully recovered with the intent of reusing all of its components. But that’s just what SpaceX is going to try to do with a Starship launch on Friday, and an upper stage landing attempt 22.5 hours later on Saturday afternoon, local time. Ars will be on hand in Starbase, Texas, to provide coverage of it all.

Electron set for milestone launch. The next launch of Rocket Lab’s Electron vehicle is due to lift off from the Mahia Peninsula in New Zealand on November 14 at 5:30 UTC. This mission will serve as Electron’s 100th overall mission—a notable milestone for the small rocket that first flew in 2017, NASASpaceflight.com reports.

A true commercial success … This flight of Electron, named “The Mountain God Rises,” will deliver another iQPS satellite into LEO for iQPS’s synthetic aperture radar constellation. The anniversary rocket features a special gold-and-black livery which looks fantastic. Rocket Lab truly does deserve recognition as the world’s second purely commercial launch business to make a successful go at it. We are all celebrating with them.

Is the Vega rocket a dead end? Last month, Italian rocket builder Avio presented a market update that outlined the additional investment required to increase the annual launch cadence of its Vega C rocket to six and eventually nine flights per year, European Spaceflight reports. The need for this additional funding raises an interesting question about the future of Italy’s Vega launch vehicle family. Is investing in the infrastructure needed to secure just three more Vega C flights per year by the end of the decade the best use of resources, particularly when several emerging European launch companies appear poised to offer comparable capacity?

Boosters may be better used by Ariane 6 … Vega C isn’t going anywhere. But it’s an inescapable reality that a single Ariane 64 launch requires the equivalent of an entire year’s worth of solid rocket boosters for Vega C at its baseline annual launch cadence of four flights. Does it make sense to continue investing in expanding Vega C’s launch cadence and later introducing Vega E when the future of both vehicles depends on production capacity that is increasingly demanded by Ariane 6?

Synspective inks 10 Alpha rockets. Firefly Aerospace announced on Tuesday a multi-launch agreement with Synspective, a Japanese synthetic aperture radar satellite data and solutions provider, for up to 10 launches on Firefly’s Alpha rocket through 2030. With the ability to launch 1,000 kg to low Earth orbit, Alpha will deliver two of Synspective’s SAR satellites to orbit per mission, deploying up to 20 total satellites.

Getting it done with one ton … Under the agreement, Firefly will launch Synspective’s SAR satellites from both Pad-0A at Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia and Space Launch Complex 2 at Vandenberg Space Force Base in California. “This landmark agreement reflects growing customer confidence in our Alpha Block II vehicle,” said Jason Kim, CEO of Firefly Aerospace. “With 1-ton payload capacity and responsive launch capabilities, Alpha is a natural fit for customers like Synspective seeking direct access to orbit to get their operations up and running sooner.”

Stoke and Planet sign multi-launch agreement. Stoke Space on Monday announced a multi-launch agreement with Planet Labs PBC. The companies did not announce the terms of the agreement, including the total number of launches under contract, nor the cost of the launches. “We need launch partners who move as quickly as we do,” said Will Marshall, Co-Founder & CEO of Planet.

An open-ended agreement? … The announcement suggests this could be an open-ended agreement, however. “Planet builds and operates the largest fleet of Earth-imaging satellites in orbit and captures imagery of Earth’s landmass every day,” a related release says. “Operators like Planet launch on a regular basis to expand and replenish their fleets, and that steady demand is what our Nova fleet is designed to serve.”

The launch crunch comes to national security. The US military is taking a new approach to prop up commercial launch companies as SpaceX looks to wind down its Falcon rocket program, suggesting defense officials see the workhorse rocket’s retirement as a potential national security risk, Ars reports. The Pentagon’s Office of Strategic Capital has twice in the last two weeks announced it was sending cash to two of SpaceX’s competitors in the launch business.

More pads and rockets … First, on October 26, the office announced a $150 million conditional loan commitment to Stoke Space. Military officials revealed a second $150 million Pentagon loan Tuesday for Rocket Lab. Stoke Space’s loan is tailored to support the buildout of the company’s second launch pad at Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, Florida. The loan to Rocket Lab will support the company’s efforts to increase the flight rate of its partially reusable Neutron rocket.

Rocket Lab raises Neutron price. Citing strong customer demand and a constrained market for launch, Rocket Lab announced this week it had increased the cost of a dedicated Neutron rocket from $55 million to $70 million, Payload reports. In recent months, the coming shortage of launch capacity in the US—mainly due to SpaceX sunsetting its Transporter rideshare program—has sparked a flurry of interest in new medium-lift rocket entrants.

Positive on Neutron … “Neutron is rapidly establishing itself as the backbone of next-generation constellation deployment and government missions in the medium-lift launch class,” CEO Peter Beck said in a statement. “Strong international demand and a growing launch manifest, at a new price point, is proof that Neutron is the launch vehicle the market has been waiting for.” The price hike for Neutron isn’t keeping customers away as new bookings continue, the company said. Customers’ next options to fly on Neutron are now in the 2030s and beyond.

Rockets are not hard? During an hour-long event on Thursday, Abel Avellan, the founder and chief executive of the telecommunications company AST SpaceMobile, laid out his vision for developing a large rocket to launch his Bluebird satellites into orbit, Ars reports. Avellan emphasized, over and over again, that AST plans to build a “simple” rocket. He paraphrased SpaceX founder Elon Musk several times, saying, “the best part is no part.”

This sounds a little naive … “Of course rocket engineers are going to get mad with me, but you can simplify it as a big tank feeding engines,” he said of the rocket. “So big tanks—we know how to do them. We know how to do them. We know how to do them in composite.” The Blacklift rocket will be “simple,” he said, because it will be solely tailored to lift five Bluebird satellites into low-Earth orbit at a time. It will have 20 metric tons of lift capacity, a reusable first stage, and launch twice a month from a pad at Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia that formerly served the Antares rocket. (submitted by thegooglemonster)

When will the Eclipse rocket be ready? During an earnings call this week (see transcript), Firefly CEO Kim spoke about the company’s ongoing work to develop the Eclipse rocket. “We’re heads down on Eclipse,” he said. “We’re laser-focused on delivering the first stage to our co-developer Northrop Grumman. And we’re over 175 hot fires on Miranda, counting the one from today. And we’re looking at all the data, verifying the capability. It’s a very mature engine, and we’re very confident in its capabilities.”

Probably not launching soon … However, when an analyst pressed on Firefly getting a launch in 2027, Kim did not offer a direct answer. “Everything is just laser-focused on all the milestones in terms of qualifying Miranda and qualifying our composite tanks and fluid systems as well,” he said. “So we’re really, really just excited about the future of Eclipse, especially with this injection of capital that will help accelerate our production line.” Bottom line: Don’t expect to see Eclipse on a launch pad any time soon.

Starship set for 15th test flight on Friday. Flight 15 of Starship is preparing to launch as soon as Friday, November 13, pending regulatory approval, SpaceX said. The 75-minute launch window will open at 4:45 p.m. CT (22:45 UTC). The upcoming flight is planned to be the first to return both Starship and Super Heavy to the launch site for catch. If successful, this will mark the first full recovery of an orbital launch system.

All eyes on the ship … Starship’s initial return-to-launch-site mission is expected to orbit the Earth at an altitude of about 275 km and complete approximately 14 orbits around the planet over the course of a more than 22-hour flight. Starship’s primary mission will be to deliver 26 Starlink V3 satellites to orbit. But really, this mission is all about the landings, first of the booster and then, more dramatically, of the upper stage Starship on Saturday.

SpaceX is now an ‘infrastructure’ company. For a long time, SpaceX has been synonymous with developing newer, larger, and ever-more-reusable rockets, Ars reports. But that began to change over the last decade with the introduction of Starlink, and the change has accelerated during the last 18 months as the company has pivoted to data and artificial intelligence. At last week’s UP.Summit conference in Florida, SpaceX’s vice president of launch, Kiko Dontchev, offered some of the most revealing comments to date about the company’s changing focus.

From AI to Mars? … “In the last couple years, when I talk about SpaceX, yes, we are a rocket company, but fundamentally we are an infrastructure company at this point,” Dontchev said. “We’re building infrastructure on the ground and low Earth orbit, soon on the Moon, and obviously eventually Mars.” However, Dontchev insisted that human settlement and Mars remain part of the core of SpaceX’s aspirations. “We have come to understand that this tool is critical to us accelerating our pace of innovation,” he said of artificial intelligence. “Without AI, I think we still would have gotten to the Moon. I think we still get to Mars with SI. We’re going to get there so much faster.”

Nov. 13: Vulcan | Amazon Leo LV-01 | Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, Florida | 17:54 UTC

Nov. 13: Starship | Flight 15 | Starbase, Texas | 22:45 UTC

Nov. 14: Soyuz 2.1b | MKA Kinosputnik, others | Vostochny Cosmodrome, Russia | 00:50 UTC

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Jeffries shredded over Hegseth impeachment snafu as Dem jockeys for speaker’s gavel

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Back-to-back blunders on the House floor are raising questions over whether Democrats are ready for primetime as they're poised to take over the majority — and Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., makes a bid for the top role.

Democrats are projected to win 255 House seats, with 12 races yet to be called, and Jeffries is running to be Speaker of the House following the sweeping victories last week that saw the party make gains even in traditional Republican strongholds like Texas, Mississippi, Kansas and South Carolina.

But some have questioned whether the recent snafus signal that Jeffries and other leaders may not be ready to be in command of the House majority.

"I may have to rethink my support of Hakeem Jeffries after the Hegseth impeachment vote," Fred Trump III, a Democrat, author and nephew of President Donald Trump, posted on X. "Eleven Democrats do not show up? All Republicans vote it down."

"Pete Hegseth is laughing at you and is stronger today. Shame," he continued. "A slap in the face of the military and veterans on this important day."

House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries speaks alongside Rep. Pete Aguilar and Rep. Katherine Clark during a news conference in Washington, D.C., on Jan. 8, 2026. (Anna Moneymaker/Getty Images)

HEGSETH THWARTS GOP-LED IMPEACHMENT PUSH AS QUESTIONS SWIRL OVER WHETHER ALLEGATIONS RISE TO HIGH CRIMES

House GOP leaders successfully blocked Rep. Thomas Massie, R-Ky., from leading the charge to impeach Secretary of War Pete Hegseth earlier this week.

Part of the reason for the failure was the absence of 11 House Democrats during a procedural vote on Tuesday evening.

Had just seven of them been present, the vote to kill Massie's impeachment resolution would have failed, triggering debate and a vote on impeaching the Cabinet secretary likely the next day.

"Everyone knew that attendance was key tonight but somehow Democratic leaders failed to deliver and handed Republicans a win," Demand Progress Executive Director Sean Vitka said in a statement.

'THE VIEW' CO-HOST CALLS DEMOCRATS MISSING HEGSETH IMPEACHMENT VOTE A 'SELF-INFLICTED WOUND'

Progressive journalist David Schuster wrote on X, "Sure, house Dems will have 259 seats in January instead of current 213. And Hegseth impeachment can be reintroduced. But Hakeem having 11 Dem absences today on Hegseth (thus losing vote) reveals a Hakeem leadership problem. Embarrassing."

An emotional Rep. Melanie Stansbury, D-N.M., said in a video posted to X that she and other Democrats were stuck in traffic when they missed the vote.

Various reports, however, said they were at a fundraiser less than two miles from the U.S. Capitol.

U.S. Secretary of War Pete Hegseth takes questions from reporters during a news conference at the Pentagon on Nov. 10, 2026, in Arlington, Va. (Chip Somodevilla/Getty Images)

HOUSE DEM MISSES HEGSETH IMPEACHMENT VOTE BECAUSE OF MAJOR MEDICAL PROCEDURE ONE WEEK AFTER ELECTION DAY

Others blamed medical procedures and family emergencies.

The day after, a bill Democratic leadership was actively urging lawmakers to vote against passed by unanimous voice vote — simply because no Democrats called for a roll call instead.

Democrats accused Republicans of parliamentary tricks, including a post on the X account belonging to the House Veterans Affairs Committee minority, "Republicans pushed it through that way intentionally so their members wouldn’t have to go on the record supporting cuts to veterans’ benefits."

But the majority of Democrats do not believe it is an early blight on Jeffries' potential speakership.

HAKEEM JEFFRIES DECLARES BID FOR HOUSE SPEAKER AFTER GOP'S MIDTERM MASSACRE

"I think there's frustration in terms of whipping votes. I'm not entirely sure where the breakdown is.  And it does seem like there's maybe some tension somewhere," Democratic strategist Mike Nellis told Fox News Digital. "I think it'll be incumbent on the incoming speaker to make sure that everybody feels like they're on the same page ... beyond that, if you're asking me if it makes a difference in a leadership flight, I mean, nobody's running against Hakeem. I haven't heard of anybody seriously thinking about doing that, so I don't know that it'll change anything."

He said House Democrats largely "have faith" in Jeffries and House Minority Whip Katherine Clark, D-Mass., who is poised to be the next House Majority Leader.

Democrat Dan Turrentine, co-host of "The Huddle," told Fox News Digital "a few Democrats unintentionally missed one vote. Unfortunately, this happens periodically in both chambers, for both parties. It does not speak to Jeffries' leadership."

And other House Democrats, when asked, directed frustration at their colleagues instead.

Rep. Melanie Stansbury, D-N.M., arrives for the House Oversight and Government Reform Committee deposition of former Attorney General Pam Bondi about the Jeffrey Epstein files, in the Rayburn building on Friday, May 29, 2026. (Tom Williams/CQ-Roll Call, Inc via Getty Images)

"My message is always to members that we need to be here and be on time," Clark told reporters Wednesday. "But there was a clear message from the Republican Party as well that they are unwilling, even after this election, to stand up against Hegseth in this war."

Rep. Jared Moskowitz, D-Fla., said, "It's unfortunate, but that's not on leadership."

Fox News Digital reached out to Jeffries' political team for comment.

The Audacious Southern Chef Teaching Angelenos to Shut Up and Listen

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Vinyl bars like West Hollywood’s Darling, from star chef Sean Brock, will help you to appreciate music, and food, and life, all over again.

In our increasingly soulless world of algorithmic playlists and AI-generated art, one of life’s great analog pleasures is listening to records with friends. A dusty old LP pre-breaks the ice for new connections, unlocks stories you haven’t heard from old ones, gives you the chance to get collectively lost in the music.

Vinyl night is such a vital human need, it’s a surprise there aren’t more third spaces built around satisfying it. Characteristically, Japan is miles ahead of America on this front. In the country’s countless kissa bars, bartenders mix drinks and spin records, and—I swear this is true—patrons keep their voices down and listen. My partner and I saw and heard it for ourselves on a trip to Japan last year: every major city has a few joints where the speakers are big, the tables have signs that say NO LOUD LAUGHING, and the customers obey. At a packed and reverently silent bar in Kyoto called PM Sounds, a few whiskeys deep and well into the AM, side one of Jeff Buckley’s Grace hit just right. “Did we really fly halfway around the world to sit and listen to an album we have at home,” I asked Ben on the walk back to the hotel. “Yes,” he replied, and we both agreed it was worth the trip.

Americans are famously not a quiet people, nor are we particularly good with being told what to do. Could kissa culture happen here? A few places in Los Angeles are rising to the challenge: The Gold Line in Highland Park has a hip crowd and shelves swelling with the massive record collection of owner Peanut Butter Wolf. Hollywood’s The Living Room is massive and meticulously acoustically treated, and it’s currently one of the tightest doors in town. But in West Hollywood, across the street from the metastasizing gay club The Abbey and steps away from the log cabin where the hip AA meetings happen, Southern super-chef Sean Brock just might have hit the sweet spot: his first California restaurant, Darling, has an eclectic menu, outstanding cocktails, and speakers that will make you feel like you’re right there in the recording studio. “Music brings people together, just like food,” Brock says. “I've been obsessed with listening bars for a while, and I've been trying to get people into them since 2023. The thrill of discovery is amazing, and I think the more AI the world gets, the more we're going to reach for this stuff to balance it out.”

Brock looks every inch both the 21st-century Generation X chef and DJ: horn-rimmed glasses, sleeve tattoos, ADHD energy. He’s the James Beard Award-winning chef behind a string of beloved Nashville restaurants past and present, including modern Southern standby Husk and two-in-one restaurants Audrey and June. He’s respected by foodies and the hoi polloi alike; ask anyone who did time in Nashville this century, and they’ll tell you a snack at the bar of a Sean Brock joint is the reward for a hard day’s work. When we first met, just before Darling opened, we bonded over his massive collection of 45s in the DJ booth that looks over the restaurant’s bar area. As a secular song from New Orleans preacher Bobby Powell faded out, he wanted to play me something. “Have you heard this?” It was a cover of the Mamas and the Papas’ “California Dreamin’” by a Jackson, Tennessee band called The Jacksonians. “This is all they ever did! They teased us with this and then never did another thing!” As it played, we paused, we nodded our heads. “This is the one. When people hear this, they stop talking.”

The DJ booth and its speaker cabinets were made custom for the space. “The key is the wood,” he said. “13-ply Baltic birch, which is impossible to get now.” Within the cabinets, massive Italian speakers, with Altec Lansing drivers upcycled from their first life in movie theaters.

When Brock is at Darling—generally on the weekdays if he’s not traveling—he plays records as the restaurant opens, takes over in the kitchen as the dinner rush starts, then hops back onto the 1s and 2s after the last dish has been served, going as long into the night as the city will let him. “Getting to play music for people after working in the kitchen all night is my reward now. For the longest time, it was getting a beer after work. Now it's just watching music bring people happiness.”

As a record collector and a chef, Brock is always thinking about the evolution of local flavor. “What I’m chasing is the knowledge of the sound and the rhythm of a city and a time,” he told me. Take Memphis in the early ‘70s, for example. “There was Willie Mitchell at Royal Studios with the Hi label doing Al Green and Anne Peebles, and you had Stax. And then there was this anger building because of the Vietnam War, people are upset, and you feel that in the music.” He grabbed a 7-inch from the pile, the gateway 45 that got him into record collecting: Blackrock’s “Yeah Yeah.” And I did feel it, because the band is a murderers’ row of ‘70s Memphis session musicians, but mostly because of those speakers. At the right volume and in the right seat, the T. Rex from Jurassic Park has nothing on Blackrock.

At its outset, Brock intended Darling to give him a new identity away from the Southern cuisine that made him famous. “I didn't want to come here and cook a bunch of stuff I'd already cooked,” he said. “And we'd been told LA wasn't interested in Southern cooking, that it would be too heavy or whatever. So we didn't cook any Southern dishes when we opened.” The next time I stopped in, once Darling had been up and running for a few months, that whole idea had been 86’d. “About three weeks in, everybody was like: Where are the grits, Sean?”

“Now this has turned into a place where people come to taste the flavors of the South that I've been working on for 20 years — all the seed saving, all the research on traditions and recipes and history. I had gotten a little bored with it honestly, but all of a sudden I have a brand new audience for whom it's completely new, and I’m completely reinvigorated.”

He was describing, almost exactly, the experience Ben and I had hearing Jeff Buckley in Japan. Whether it’s food or music, there’s something revelatory about paying close attention, even to something you think you know.

So that’s the food, and I can tell you from experience that the steak burger is worth the trip (and make that trip early; he only makes 24 a night). As for the music, Brock believes in the power of the listening bar with the zeal of the recent kissa convert. “I had a kind of life-changing moment in a listening bar around 2022,” he says. “I was sitting in the sweet spot — the tip of the triangle, equidistant from two gigantic Tannoys, and the sound became 3D. It floated. I stopped drinking nine years ago, but I thought they'd accidentally given me alcohol.” He got on Nashville Craigslist that very night, searched “Tannoy,” and sent a message to a seller, who wrote back “I ate in your restaurant last night.” It’s good to be the king.

Brock has seats in the bar area of Darling situated at the perfect distance and angle from the speakers to give customers an out of body experience if they want it. But like a legendary Southern chef distancing himself from grits, a listening bar in Los Angeles is a risky concept. In Japan, the locals and tourists know to keep quiet and focus on the music, but this is West Hollywood, where everybody wants to be the thing that is focused on. Where if people have a song selector they trust, it’s usually their Peloton instructor. As an Angeleno, I have to worry whether anyone will pipe down long enough to pay attention. “There's a difference between a night where you come to eat and drink and socialize while music is playing, and a night where we're saying: what we're playing is special and rare. I want to test that theory soon,” Brock told me. He’s been promoting more and more listening sessions: tributes to jazz record labels Strata-East and International Anthem, a vinyl party hosted by Sylvan Esso, Dolly Parton’s first twelve albums, in order, on those THX-tested speakers.

Even the untrained ear can hear it coming together. I swing through again to hear local legend and NTS radio host DJ Morse Code spin a set of ‘70s and ‘80s jazz fusion and R&B. The restaurant is busy and buzzing, there’s a nice crowd up front in the good speaker seats, at the top of the triangle. Some of the folks up front are quieting down to hear the music, even as more people streamed in, even as the new arrivals were striking their poses, even as we realize that Anderson.Paak is at the bar, that Malibu cult singer-songwriter Ned Doheny is at a table in front of the DJ booth, that Kate Pierson is in the restaurant.

Darling is heating up, and the flavors, the sound, and the rhythm are continuing to evolve. The chef and the city are both learning to listen.

These smart home lights have stood the test of time

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Over the nine years since I've owned my home, I've had hundreds of smart home devices come in and out of my house. However, there's been one device that's been here nearly the entire time, and has held up pretty well for more than half a decade.

Since I reviewed them in 2020, the Ring Solar Pathlights have been faithfully keeping my front walkway illuminated through blazing summers, rainy springs, and multiple blizzards, where they were covered in more than two feet of snow.

And right now, you can get them on sale in an early Black Friday deal for around 30% off. which makes it a good time to pick them up if you want smart lighting for your home.

The Ring Solar Pathlights do a great job illuminating your walk, and they can be linked with other smart home devices, such as home security cameras and video doorbells.

Among the best solar lights, Ring's lights are handy in that you can link them to other smart home devices. For instance, if a Ring security camera or doorbell detects motion, you can program the lights to turn on automatically.

Conversely, the lights also have their own motion sensors, which can be used to trigger other smart home devices. I don't have anything so complex, but I have the lights set so that they turn on to a low setting in the evening, but then brighten if they detect motion.

Right now, you can find a few different deals on Ring's solar-powered lights:

Ring also sells battery versions of its Pathlights which are slightly cheaper, but I would advise going for the solar-powered models; you don't want to bother with having to recharge the lights yourself, and in the six years I've had the lights, I've never had an issue with them running out of juice.

The one caveat is that the batteries in two of the lights have died, as it looks like water somehow seeped in, and Ring doesn't sell replacement batteries. There are a few threads on Reddit and Ring's site about this, and owners have found that the Nitecore NL1823 18650 battery ($9 each) works as a suitable replacement.
